A spy with the task "scout" shows the squads of the target kingdom for `spyRevealMinutes`. When a squad leaves the sight, a pale mark "last seen" stays with the time (`lastSeenMinutes`). Own and allied squads are always seen. Sight is counted on the server every `fogSeconds`, only for maps that are open; a client gets only what its kingdom sees, and only a squad in sight or last seen can be attacked.
- **Trophies.** A soldier who kills a soldier of another kingdom gathers the gear that fell from the fallen (nothing else lying around) and carries it to a storehouse of its kingdom; killed on the way, it drops the gear again and the gear can be taken back. Brought in, the gear is kept as a trophy with the kingdom it belonged to. After peace that kingdom may buy its trophies back for `trophyBuybackPercent` (50%) of their worth within `trophyBuybackDays` (7) game days: a message with a "[Buy back]" button, the list `/kingdom trophies`, the buyback `/kingdom trophies buy <number>` (the right "Treasury"); the money goes from treasury to treasury by the books. What is not bought back goes to the storehouses of the winner. A player who kills a soldier picks the gear up as usual.
- **No attacks on the absent.** Soldiers leave players who are away or offline alone; the land of a kingdom under the protection of the absent cannot be attacked or besieged from the map.
- **Tactics of squads** (stage 14). Near players a squad in a fight has a leader: an officer, a sergeant or its most experienced fighter. It looks at the fight every `decideTicks` ticks (a captain 10, a vice-captain 12, a sergeant 15, a squad without an officer 20), not at once but `reactionTicks` (8) after an enemy shows itself, and sees only enemies in the line of sight of someone of the squad within `sight` (24) blocks. It gives out roles and the fighters carry them out with their own goals. Against one enemy the blades ring it: each its own sector at `ringRadius` (2.2) blocks, the first facing the squad, the others coming round its sides and back without running through it, each striking from its own side. An enemy in a narrow way or a house is awaited at its ways out at `exitRadius` (4.5) instead of going in one by one; an enemy on a pillar or a ledge is awaited at its foot until the archers bring it down. Against a group (from `pincerFoes`, 2, enemies and twice as many fighters) a squad with an officer or a sergeant holds a front (shields first) and sends wings `flankRadius` (4) blocks to the sides of the group, under an officer round to its back (a pincer); without an officer it just rings the nearest. A blow of a fighter of a squad from the side lands `flankBonusPercent` (15) percent harder, from the back `rearBonusPercent` (30). The leader chooses the target of the whole squad: the most dangerous against how quickly it falls, and one already striking its own (a captain weighs the distance better); without an officer the nearest. Archers stand on an arc `archerMin`..`archerMax` (8-15) blocks from their target, move along it until no friend stands in the line of fire, prefer higher ground, shoot those who fight their own, and step back to the far end of their range when an enemy comes closer than `archerTooClose` (4). A badly wounded fighter (below `woundedPercent`, 50 percent of its health, as citizens count) falls back `fallBack` (6) blocks behind the front with a shield bearer covering it; no more than half the squad falls back, and the wounded go to be cared for after the fight. A squad that lost `retreatLossesPercent` (50) percent of its fighters or is worth less than `retreatBalancePercent` (40) percent of its enemies falls back in order towards help, the post of its order or the capital, shields last; guards falling back from an offender bring the next step of the relief at once and come back with it. Nobody is sent into lava, fire, water or over a drop: a place to stand is checked by its blocks as the search of paths checks it. Robbers fight more simply: they lie in wait crouched until their prey comes within 8 blocks, one of them is hurt or 30 seconds pass, then ring it together and run back to their camp when the enemy is stronger. Adventurers fight the same way, an experienced one (rank C and above) leading as a sergeant. The decisions of all squads share `budgetMicros` (3000) microseconds of a tick, the rest decide in the next tick; paths come from the shared budget of paths, and close by a fighter steps to its place without a search. `enabled = false` in `[tactics]` brings back the old fight of each on its own. The command `admin army tactics` shows the squads fighting by plan, their shape and roles and the time of the decisions.

### Energy and the mage tower

- **The energy of a kingdom.** Every kingdom has a store of energy (a whole number that never goes below zero) with a journal of energy in and out by kind for today, yesterday and the week. Energy only comes from monster crystals (and from operators); neither money nor Forge Energy turns into it. The store holds `baseCapacity` plus `accumulatorCapacity` for each crystal accumulator on the kingdom's land; an accumulator taken away keeps the energy, but no more comes in until there is room.
- **Mages and the magic focus.** The mage works at a work post with a magic focus in its zone. Carriers bring crystals from the storehouses into the post's container (`crystalsAtPost`); the mage dissolves them at the focus one by one in `dissolveTicks` (faster with experience and mood), and each crystal gives exactly its energy: the crystal leaves the container and the energy enters the store in the same moment, or nothing happens. Without a container the mage takes crystals from the storehouses directly. Far from players mages work abstractly by the same rules. Near players mages defend the land: a fireball at a monster on the kingdom's land and healing for hurt neighbors (`mageFireballCost`, `mageHealCost` from the energy). The focus works anywhere on the territory; the tower template comes with the buildings.
- **Research.** Protective Ward, Healing, Fertility, Fair Wind, Gates (after the Ward) and Arcana I-III. A research costs energy at once (`researchCost`) and takes the work of mages (`researchMinutes` of one mage of usual speed; the working mages of the kingdom add up). Every finished research raises the tower by one level. The right `manage_magic` (the king and the court mage by default) runs it.
- **Spells of the kingdom** (the "Magic" tab, `spellPrice`): the protective ward (fewer natural spawns of hostile monsters in the chosen chunks, share `wardReduction`, priced per chunk and day, up to `wardMaxChunks`), healing (wounded in the infirmary get `healingPerSecond` more health per second, priced once per wounded), fertility (fields and young animals around farms and herds grow faster within `fertilityRadius`, priced per post and day), the fair wind (a caravan saves `tailwindShare` of its remaining way, priced once per caravan), gates (travel between gates for energy). Spells paid by the day pay when switched on and at the start of every day; without enough energy a spell switches off with a message, nothing is owed.
- **Gates.** The magic gate block on the kingdom's land (placed by a member with `manage_magic`, up to `maxGates`; gates and generators placed any other way, by a machine, a command, a player without the right or on land of nobody, stay inert blocks until a member with the right places them, and they stop when the land changes hands). A subject opens a gate and chooses where to go; the trip is paid with energy. Who may use the gates is decided by `KingdomWorldApi.setGateAccess` (today the kingdom's own subjects; the alliances of stage 9 can replace it).
- **Magic of players.** A staff with slots (`staffSlots`). At a focus a subject learns spells for a fee to the treasury (`lessonPrice`); strong ones need a guild rank (`spellRank`) and a tower level (`spellTowerLevel`). Fireball, healing, light, detect monsters, shield (absorption), a short blink (into the protected land of another kingdom only after a preparation of `blinkPrepareSeconds`, 8 s: stand still, a blow or a step breaks it, a bar above the hotbar; in one's own land and on nobody's land at once). A learned spell goes into a slot of the staff at the focus; using the staff casts the chosen spell, sneaking and using it chooses the next one. Each cast spends the energy of the crystals in the inventory (`spellCost`): started ones first, then the weakest; a crystal that gave part of its energy remembers what is left and stacks only with the same. Cooldowns are `spellCooldownTicks`. In peacetime spells hurt no other player and no citizen, foreign or own (exemptions of the access policy, such as war, will change that); the fireball sets no blocks on fire.
- **The rank of the guild of mages and the levels of spells** (stage 14). The guild of mages of a kingdom has a rank. It sets the level up to which it teaches the spells of players (`lessonLevelByRank`: F-B level I, A up to II, S up to III, SS and SSS up to IV); the lesson of the next level is taken by the same click at the focus, and the level is kept for the player (older data reads as level I). Every level is stronger (`levelPower`: 1, 1.4, 1.8, 2.3 times: the damage of the fireball, healing, the reach of a blink, the time of light, detection and the shield, the radius of detection; from level III the shield holds more) and dearer (`levelPrice`: 1, 2.5, 6, 15 prices of the lesson). One who is not a subject pays `foreignerPrice` (2) times the price; one wanted in that kingdom or a subject of a kingdom at war with it is not taught (`NOT_WELCOME`). The rank of the player in the guild and the level of the tower are asked for as before. Mages grow stronger with the rank of their guild: health `mageHealth` (20-130), fireball and healing `mageSpellPower` (1-2.5 times). The guild of mages of a kingdom of players is ranked once a minute by `researchWorth` (3) for a research, `mageWorth` (1) for a mage at work and `towerWorth` (2) for a working mage tower against the thresholds `guildScore` (2, 4, 7, 11, 16, 22, 30, 40 for E-SSS). The rank shows in the Magic tab and at the focus with the levels of the lessons.
- **Machines.** The crystal generator on the kingdom's land (placed by a member with `manage_magic`) gives the kingdom's energy to the blocks next to it as Forge Energy (the `ENERGY` capability): `feRate` FE per unit, at most `feMaxPerTick` per tick, units are taken from the store only for what a machine asks. Nothing goes the other way. It works with any mod that uses FE, for example Create Crafts & Additions.
- **Iron's Spells 'n Spellbooks and Ars Nouveau** (when installed, `ironsWell`, `arsWell`). Every `wellIntervalTicks` a focus refills the Iron's mana of subjects within `wellRadius` through its API (`MagicData`, the max mana attribute) and the Ars Nouveau mana (`IManaCap`), and fills the source of Ars Nouveau jars (`ISourceTile`) for the kingdom's energy at `ironsManaPerEnergy`, `arsManaPerEnergy`, `arsSourcePerEnergy`; at most `wellMaxEnergy` per player or jar and pulse. Totals go to the log once a minute. Limits: Iron's sends the new mana to the client itself with its regeneration (up to half a second later); Ars Nouveau knows a player's maximum mana only after the player's first tick, so the stand-in player of the `wells` command may get none.
- **The "Magic" tab** of the throne screen: the store and its capacity, accumulators, crystals in the storehouses, the journal for today, yesterday and the week, the mages with experience and speed, research with start buttons, spells of the kingdom with their price and switches (and the ward over the chunk the player stands in), gates and generators. The focus screen: spells to learn with their requirements and the slots of the staff in hand. The gate screen: where the gates lead and what a trip costs.

### Companions and parties of players

- **Parties.** A member of the guild invites other members into a party: the party window (key P) or `/kingdom party invite <player>`. An invitation holds for `inviteTicks`, a party has up to `maxParty` players. The leader removes members, hands over the lead, chooses how rewards are split (evenly or by shares from 0 to 100) and turns friendly fire on or off (off by default: members and their companions do not hurt each other). A leader who leaves hands the lead to the member who joined first.
- **Contracts of a party.** A contract the leader takes becomes the party's; it may be taken by the guild's rule for the leader's rank (the own rank and one above). Kills of every member and of their companions count, and any member hands in items for a gathering in parts. The reward less the branch's commission and the points are split by the shares: everybody gets the whole part, the rest goes one coin at a time to those who lost most to rounding; the parts always add up to the reward. A part an account cannot take goes to the taker, and if the taker cannot take it either, the reward goes back to whoever posted it. A member who leaves takes the contracts they hold along.
- **The list of the party** in the top left corner of the screen: the members with their health (the leader marked with a star, those offline grey), then the player's companions; on a narrow screen extra lines fold into "and N more". The client config turns it off (`showPartyList`). Party chat: `/party <text>`.
- **The slave market and the hire.** The tabs "Market" and "Hire" of the desk of the guild, for members of the guild only. Every `refreshDays` days the branch offers `slaveOffers` slaves and `freeOffers` free companions: the name in both alphabets, level, strength, health, skills (fighting, carrying, stamina) and the origin in one line. A slave costs `pricePerPower` per point of strength (at least `minPrice`), a free companion asks `wagePerPower` a day (at least `minWage`). Payment is from the account or with the coins carried; the money goes to the treasury of the branch's kingdom. How many companions a player keeps depends on the rank in the guild (`rankLimits`, by default F 1 ... SSS 5). The king closes the slave market on the kingdom's land with a button of the tab; free companions are still hired.
- **Body and record.** The record of a companion on the server is the truth, as for citizens. The body exists while the owner is online and near: a companion that follows appears next to its owner and goes with it to other worlds, one told to stand or guard appears at its place when the owner comes within `bodyRange` blocks. When the owner leaves, the body goes and the record and the pack stay; while the owner is away, the companion waits, neither getting hungry nor being paid.
- **Orders and fighting.** Follow, stand, guard the place, go on a contract (hunt the monsters of the owner's and the party's contracts nearby). A companion fights like a soldier of the army: a melee weapon by damage and speed, a bow or crossbow of any mod with arrows from the pack, a shield and armor; it takes the best of its pack itself, and wear goes to the piece in the pack.
- **The pack** in the window of the companion (right click your companion): 9 slots at carrying 1-3, 18 at 4-6, 27 at 7-9, 36 at 10. The items live in the record, not in the body, so the owner leaving, a restart and the death of the body never copy them; when a companion dies, the pack falls where it died.
- **Food, experience, healing.** Food falls by one every `hungerTicks` (stamina slows it). A companion eats on its own when its food is at `eatBelow` or lower: from the pack first, then from the inventory of its owner nearby; it eats plain food, never harmful food. After `starveTicks` without food it grows weaker (`starvingStrength`) and loses loyalty. Kills of monsters give experience; a level adds health and raises the lowest skill. Wounds heal slowly (`healTicks`), faster at an infirmary of the owner's kingdom (`infirmaryHealTicks`).
- **Loyalty** (0-100), the reasons shown in the window. It falls with long hunger, heavy wounds, the owner's blows and unpaid days; it rises with good keeping (fed and healthy; a slave up to `wellKeptCap` this way), gifts (Shift and right click with an item in hand, by the item's worth) and freedom. Below `escapeBelow` a companion warns (a message and a "!" in the window and in the list). If loyalty does not rise again within `escapeTicks` after the warning, a slave runs away with its whole pack (by default it vanishes; the stage of roads and robbers can send it to a camp of robbers through `CompanionWork.setEscapeRoute`), and a free companion leaves and gives back what it carried.
- **Free companions** serve for pay: at the start of a day, while the owner is online, the wage goes from the owner's account to the treasury of the branch. Unpaid, the companion loses loyalty and leaves after `unpaidDays` days, giving back what it carried. The window lets it go ("Let go").
- **Freedom and sale.** A slave set free stays as a free companion with loyalty of at least `freedLoyalty` and asks a daily wage; an owner who is a subject of a kingdom may instead let it settle as a citizen of that kingdom under the same name. A slave can be sold back to the market of the branch where it was bought for `sellPercent`% of its price from the treasury, when the treasury has the money; it shows up among the offers again. The pack goes back to the owner.
- **Death** is final: the chronicle of the owner's kingdom (or of the branch where the companion was bought) remembers it, and the pack falls where it died.
- **Peace.** Companions never hurt players, citizens or other companions, by a blow or by a stray arrow, and never touch players who are away from the keyboard or offline; other players cannot hurt somebody else's companion. The court of a kingdom forbids slavery (see "Law and guards"): the slaves of its subjects and foreign slaves on its land go free.

### Law and guards

- **Crimes.** A crime for whoever lacks the right: taking from a container of others (the storehouses of the kingdom, the chests in the houses of citizens, chests of players; a container a player placed on the land of a kingdom is theirs), harvesting ripe fields of others, killing, shearing or milking the herds of the kingdom (the "Build" right), striking a citizen, killing a citizen or a player, breaking a block in a city of the kingdom or a block of its building, wall or road (damaged property, the "Build" right). No crime for the king and the officials of the kingdom (the nobles of stage 10 join through `LawPolicy.setNobility`), the owner of the thing, whoever holds the right, an enemy in war (`LawPolicy.addExemption`), an operator with bypass and machines of mods. Subjects answer like strangers. A theft counts at the market price of the kingdom or the worth of the item; thefts from one victim, blocks broken one after another and blows at one victim within `mergeSeconds` make one crime. Companions of players commit no crimes.
- **Damaged property.** The property of a kingdom is every block inside one of its cities (the square of the wall with the wall itself, and without a wall the square a wall would take at the level of the city) and every block of its registered building, wall or road anywhere on its land. A block of property an outsider breaks drops no item, is written down as it stood (for streets, gardens and freehand builds what was there is kept) and comes back for free, `SiegeRepair.PER_SECOND` (4) blocks a second, once there was no new damage for `restoreQuietSeconds` (30) seconds or the offender left the land. As the block comes back for free it cannot be carried away: the loop "break, wait, break" gives nothing. Whatever cannot stay without the block goes with it (a torch, a rail, a carpet, a door or a crop on it, sand and gravel over it, the other half of a block of two, and on along the chain): without items too, written down after it and put back after its support. Damaged property gives no item and loses no block. A block does not break at all when an item frame or a painting hangs on it, when it holds up a block with data or more than 64 blocks (`LawVandalism.CASCADE`), and while the book of damage of the kingdom is full or cannot be written: what cannot be written down is not broken, and old records do not give way to new ones. A block an outsider places in a city stands until the place is put in order, then it is taken out and falls as an item; outsiders may take their own block back themselves. The fine is `vandalismFinePercent` (200) percent of the worth of what was broken times the multiplier of the court; a witness is needed as for any crime, while putting back needs none. Witnesses are looked for once a second for an offender, not for every block. Outside the cities, on the plain land of a kingdom, blocks are broken and placed freely: no crime, nothing put back. In war the old rules hold (open land, mending from the storehouses). At most `WarDamage.MAX_PER_KINGDOM` (16384) records per kingdom.
- **The goods of citizens and the treasury.** The state has its stock, the citizens have theirs. The stock of a kingdom lies in its storehouses (the storehouse block and the chests of warehouse buildings); a theft from them is recorded as a theft from the treasury and fined harder (`treasuryFinePercent`, 150 percent of the fine for a common theft). A soldier within `storehouseWatchBlocks` (8) blocks of a storehouse notices a theft at once, without seeing the thief: wanted and an alarm with no delay. The chest of a house holds the goods of those who live there: of every wage paid the citizen takes `householdSharePercent` (20) percent home, the rest leaves the world as before. When the house is loaded this money buys a little food from the storehouses of the kingdom (`2` items at a time, up to `householdFood` (6) in the house, and only while the storehouses hold more than four items of food per citizen; the price goes back to the treasury), and the rest is laid into the chest as coins of the kingdom up to `householdCoinsMax` (300); what goes beyond leaves the world. Nothing is made out of nothing. Taking from the chest of a house is a theft from the citizen: the record names the citizen. The chests of houses are no stock of the kingdom: citizens eat from the storehouses, the market sells from the storehouses.
- **Witnesses.** Citizens, soldiers and subjects online and at the keyboard within `witnessRadius` and in sight. A soldier reports at once; a citizen is frightened and runs, and the report arrives after `reportMinSeconds`..`reportMaxSeconds` seconds if any witness is still alive. The line "A witness ran to the guards!" is shown only when a witness did run to a soldier of its kingdom (one within 48 blocks and not behind the offender). The victim is a witness only when it lives and saw who struck it; the dead tell nothing. Nobody sees through walls: whoever only heard a blow (`fearHearRadius`) is frightened and runs, but does not know who it was and makes nobody wanted. Nobody saw it: there is no record and no alarm, and a soldier who sees the player later does not put that crime on the record.
- **Fright.** A blow at a citizen or a soldier frightens the peaceful citizens with a body: the one struck, everybody who sees it within `fearSightRadius` (16) blocks and everybody who hears it within `fearHearRadius` (10) blocks without a line of sight (the noise wakes sleepers). Each one who starts to run frightens the calm ones within `fearSpreadRadius` (5) blocks once, and those do not spread it further, so a massacre causes no avalanche of searches. A frightened citizen drops its work and its day and runs from the threat: home and inside or to a soldier when the way there does not lead past the attacker, otherwise simply away; the rule "no farther than 32 blocks from home" is lifted for that time. The fright lasts `fearSeconds` (18) seconds after the last cause and a new cause makes it last longer; at night whoever was woken does not lie down while frightened. Soldiers, guards and adventurers do not run, they fight. The fright asks no law: a player in any game mode (creative too), a monster, an enemy soldier and a king who beats his own people all frighten. Free citizens (without work, after their hours and on a festival) stroll near their home.
- **The alarm.** A blow at a citizen or a soldier and a killing that somebody saw (the victim who lived, a witness, a soldier) raise the alarm of the kingdom at once, with the offender and the place: nobody waits for a report. Every soldier of the kingdom with a body in the city of the place and within 16 blocks beyond its wall, or within `alarmRadius` (64) blocks of the place, runs there: guards with an order and soldiers without one from the barracks alike. The place of the alarm is where a soldier (32 blocks) or a citizen (`witnessRadius`) last saw the offender in a line of sight; nobody follows the offender through walls. A soldier that does not see the offender goes to that place and searches there. The alarm ends `alarmSeconds` (60) seconds after the last sighting, or when the offender died, paid or went further than 48 blocks beyond the wall or the edge of a city of the kingdom: there is no chase across the huge land of a state, the soldiers go back to their posts and the record stays (the bounty of the guild and the next visit to the city deal with it). A soldier that sees the offender is a witness itself: the reports witnesses still carry are recorded at once, even if those witnesses are dead. While blows keep falling (`ongoingSeconds`, 10 seconds after a blow) and after a killing the guards strike without calling out; for petty matters and for whoever stopped striking they still call out. A soldier a player strikes is a witness of that blow: it is an assault on a guard even if nobody else saw it, and the soldier answers at once. A wounded soldier does not walk off to be cared for while an alarm lasts close by. A theft from a storehouse next to a soldier raises the alarm too, without the right to strike at once. With no soldier body near, the record waits as before. The state of an alarm (the offender, the place, when it began and how long it lasts, whether somebody was killed, how many soldiers the offender killed and wounded) comes from `LawAlarms.active`: the army decides on reinforcements from it.
- **Relief.** An alarm is answered step by step (`[army.force]`). The fighters of the guard run at once. The vice-captain of the guard comes when two soldiers are killed or wounded (`viceLosses`) or the fight lasts 20 seconds (`viceSeconds`); the captain of the guard: four out (`captainLosses`), the vice-captain killed or badly wounded, or 40 seconds (`captainSeconds`); the royal army with its vice-captain and half of its fighters (`armyFirstShare`, the nearest to the place): six out (`armyLosses`), the captain of the guard out, or 60 seconds (`armySeconds`); the captain of the army with everybody left: ten out (`allLosses`) or the vice-captain of the army out. An officer is out when it is dead or below `officerOutPercent` (40) percent of its health. A fight counts from the first blow: an alarm for a theft alone calls nobody but the guard. The army of a kingdom without a guard answers at once. A step once reached is kept until the alarm ends; then everybody goes back to the posts and the barracks. The offender sees one line when the army comes out. A soldier who is struck answers by itself, whatever step the alarm is at. The bodies of soldiers an alarm calls appear before those of other citizens while bodies are scarce (`maxActiveNpcs`). In a war and a siege everything is as before, officers fight with everybody else.
- **The wanted** are kept by each kingdom: the crimes and the fine. Petty crimes (theft, assault, trespass) are forgotten after `pettyDays` game days (twice as fast with a high reputation, half again as slow with a low one), murder never. A chat message only when the player becomes wanted or is no longer; while the player stands on the land of a kingdom that wants them, one line above the hotbar names the kingdom and the fine or the death.
- **Guards** are soldiers of the army whose squad holds a point, guards an area or patrols (and soldiers guarding an area at the gates of cities). A guard that sees a wanted player on the land of its kingdom calls out; a right click on a soldier opens "Give up and pay N". Who neither gives up in `hailSeconds` nor leaves the land is attacked. For petty crimes guards beat but do not kill: the blow that would kill leaves `beatenHealth` health and a short weakness, and the fine is taken at once (twice the worth of a theft, `assaultFine`, `trespassFine`, all times the multiplier of the court): coins of the kingdom from the inventory with change, then the account, the rest a debt to the treasury. For a murder guards kill: before anything falls, everything in the inventory and the armor goes into the confiscated goods of the kingdom (a record in the data of the law, not a chest in the world), coins of the kingdom into its treasury. Jack's Gravestones (modid `jacksgravestones`) then makes no grave: the inventory is empty. With `keepInventory` the execution takes the goods all the same (the sentence does not depend on the rules of death) and nothing is left to keep. Guards never touch players away from the keyboard or offline, nor spectators; the record waits. A player in creative mode is answered like any other (`pursueCreative`, on by default): guards and soldiers run up, surround and strike. The game does such a player no harm, so nothing is taken, while the crimes are recorded as usual; the chase ends when the player left the land or the alarm ran out. An operator clears the record of a player with `/kingdomworld admin law pardon <player> <kingdom>`.
- **Ransom.** The owner buys the goods back whole for `ransomPercent` (50) percent of their worth, with coins or from the account, at the throne, a barracks block, a working watchtower or barracks building of that kingdom, or at the town hall of one of its cities. Exactly those goods come back; what does not fit falls at the owner's feet. Goods nobody claimed in `confiscationDays` days go to the storehouses of the kingdom. Goods of a kingdom that is gone are given back for free anywhere.
- **The court.** The "Law" tab of the throne screen, right "Court" (`manage_law`): the king and the chancellor; the chancellor of an old world gets it once (`law_rights`). The wanted with their record (hint over the name), "Pardon", "Fine" (only lower) and "Spare" (the death is replaced by the fine `murderFine`); the laws: the multiplier of fines, the entry fee of large cities, the reward for heads, the ban of slavery. A subject sees their own record, reputation, debt and confiscated goods in the tab.
- **Hunters of heads.** For every wanted murderer the branch of the guild of the kingdom posts a contract "Head of a criminal" with a reward from the treasury (`bountyReward` or the court's reward, 0 turns it off) for `bountyDays` days and posts it anew while the murderer is wanted. A member of the guild who kills the wanted player online and at the keyboard does the contract: it is an execution (confiscation, record cleared), not a murder. A pardon or an execution by the guards withdraws the contract; the escrow goes back to the treasury. A hunter and the target in one party of the guild get no reward; the stake goes back. From the third head of the same player one hunter collects, the reward is the least of three: the usual reward, the ransom of the goods taken, the coins taken (for example coins 2000, goods worth 3000 with a ransom of 1500: the reward is 1500), so the kingdom never loses; the count of heads is kept in the data of the law.
- **Reputation** from -100 to 100 in each kingdom. Crimes lower it (once per crime); trade on the market of the kingdom (`tradePerPoint`), a tax or entry fee paid in full (`taxReputation`) and contracts of the guild done there (`contractReputation`) raise it. It moves market prices for the player by up to `priceEffectPercent` (10) percent either way; from `highReputation` cities let the player in for free; petty records fade faster.
- **Debts to a treasury** are paid back from income in the kingdom's currency by themselves (sales to the market and of lots, rewards of the guild, payments of the treasury, transfers from players; coins handed in at a bank are no income) and with "Pay off" in the window of the law (from the account, then with coins). Guards never attack for a debt. With a debt above `debtLimit` the market of the kingdom sells to the player only for coins and takes no new lots. No interest.
- **Entry into cities.** Guards at a gate check who comes in: a wanted player is called out and kept out. A large city asks an entry fee (the court's, up to `maxEntryFee`) from players who are no subjects, officials or respected; the price moves a little with reputation. Paid once, the pass holds a game day in every city of the kingdom. One button in the window at the gate (coins or account) pays it, or the player pays by themselves if "Pay entry myself" is on in the window of the law; without paying, the guards politely name the price and keep the player out. The entry follows `CityEntryEvent` of the roads: through a gate of the wall the entry is checked (`LawEntry.onEnter`; a soldier guarding an area near the gate turns to the newcomer, without one the check is made all the same), and a player kept out is put back outside the gate with the price; a large city is `CityLevel.CITY` (`CityWalls.level`), the gates are `CityWalls.gates`. Who gets in past the gates (over the wall, through a gap) and so avoids the fee or being stopped commits a trespass when seen; subjects are free in their own cities. Nobody stands at the gate of a city without a wall.
- **The ban of slavery** (the "Law" tab): the slaves of the kingdom's subjects go free, a foreign slave on its land goes free as a loyal free companion (by the rules of companions) and the owner pays `slaveryFine` to the treasury (offline: as a debt). An owner with slaves is warned once per approach, `slaveryWarnBlocks` blocks before the border. The slave market of such a kingdom is closed.
- **The window of the law** (key J, `/kingdom law`): by kingdom the record, reputation, debt with "Pay off" and the pass; confiscated goods with their ransom, days left and where they are bought back; the switch to pay entry by oneself.

### Roads and robbers

- **Cities.** A town hall (block) placed on the land of a kingdom by a member with the "Construction" right (`manage_construction`) founds a city named on an anvil, or after the kingdom with a number. At most `maxCities` cities, no closer than `minCityDistance` blocks to another city or a capital. The capital counts as a city. Breaking the town hall gives the city up; its roads stay. Using the town hall names its city.
- **Planning.** "Plan a road" in the Roads tab opens the atlas for a road: two clicks set the ends (within 48 blocks of a city or a capital an end sticks to it), and the server searches the way over the land (A*, up to `searchStepsPerTick` steps and `searchChunksPerTick` new chunks a tick, at most `maxRouteLength` blocks and `maxSearchSteps` steps). A road goes up or down one block a step, so it goes round cliffs and climbs mountains in serpentines; it keeps away from lava, fire, cacti, magma and what must not be broken (hardness below zero, the tag `kingdomworld:unclearable`, the blocks of the mod, containers and any block entity); over water it crosses on a bridge at the level of the water, over a shallow gap on an embankment up to `maxFill`, over a deep one on a bridge; no bridge or embankment longer than `maxBridge`. Foreign land costs more (`foreignCost`), so the way goes round it when that costs little. The atlas shows the way, its length, bridges and embankments, the materials and time for the chosen class and the kingdoms on the way; "Build" turns the plan into a road.
- **Classes:** a path (1 block; grass and earth become a path, no materials), a gravel road (3 blocks of gravel from the tag `kingdomworld:road_gravel`), a paved road (3 blocks of stone from `kingdomworld:road_paving`). Bridges are made of `kingdomworld:road_bridge` (planks), embankments of `kingdomworld:road_fill` (earth, cobblestone). The tags take blocks of mods.
- **Building.** A road is cut into sections of 16 blocks. Builders of the kingdom with no construction within reach of their post work on its roads: every block is paid for from the storehouses (what other work set aside stays) at the pace of the class (`secondsPerBlock`), the paid items wait in the section until the block is in the world, and a block is placed as soon as its place is loaded, so far from players a section counts as built once it is paid for. Replaced blocks give their drops to the storehouses; grass and flowers over the road go. What must not be broken, containers and block entities stay: that block of road is left out and its items go back. A road given up gives its unused items back.
- **Between kingdoms.** Wild land is free to build on. If the way touches the land of another kingdom, that kingdom gets a request (like a deal) to answer in the Roads tab within `crossingDays` days. Until every kingdom agrees the road waits; a refusal or silence gives it up. Consent is remembered (`rememberConsent`): once an owner let a road of a kingdom "at our cost" through, the next such roads over its land are built without a new request; a joint road, where the owner builds and pays on its own land, always asks it again. A joint road: every kingdom builds the sections on its land with its builders and materials, the planner builds those on wild land; "at our cost": the planner builds all of it. A block of road is never placed on the land of an owner who did not agree, even if it is paid for.
- **Wear and repair.** Built sections wear very slowly (`wearPerDay` by class, faster on busy roads). A worn road is slower for caravans. Builders repair sections whose condition fell below `repairBelow` by themselves (`autoRepair`, on by default); a repair can also be ordered in the tab. A repair takes `repairShare` of the materials of a section.
- **Speed.** A player on a built road (standing on a block of road, not on the grass beside it) is faster by `playerSpeed` for its class, a ridden horse by `horseSpeed`; `speedOnRoads = false` turns it off.
- **Caravans.** A caravan takes the fastest way through the network of roads: on built sections it goes `caravanSpeed` times faster than across country (`caravanBlocksPerMinute`), from the end of a road it changes to roads nearby, across country it goes straight. The way is kept and survives restarts; the atlas and the map mods move the caravan along it. Near players (`physicalCaravans`) the caravan walks the road in the world: a merchant with one or two pack llamas (they cannot be taken, drop nothing and players cannot hurt them), and the guards of the army walk beside it; far from players it is abstract. The goods are always in the data of the caravan; the bodies only show where it is.
- **Camps of robbers** appear once a day with the chance `campSpawnChance` (at most `maxCamps`) in the wild (at least 400 blocks from the claimed land of kingdoms of the world, `campMinDistance` from the claims of kingdoms of players and 128 blocks from roads and other camps; the provinces of states keep no distance, their wilds are where robbers live), more likely near busy roads, and gain a robber a day (`campGrowthChance`) up to `campMaxBandits` while nobody disturbs them. Near players a camp stands in the world (a fire, two tents, a chest), built only where nothing would be broken, and its robbers walk about it. The blocks of a camp drop nothing when broken; the chest gives only the loot. A slave who escapes its owner runs to the nearest camp of its dimension and becomes a robber, and its pack becomes loot of the camp; with no camp it is gone with its pack.
- **A robber** (a mob of its own, the leader stronger) takes weapons and armor from the tables `banditWeapons` and `banditArmor` (items and tags of any mod with weights), shoots if it has a bow, follows the goals of vanilla mobs, breaks no blocks and picks up nothing, drops nothing and leaves alone players who are away from the keyboard.
- **Ambushes** are rare and can be tuned. Far from players every piece of the way near a camp (within `campReach`) may hold an ambush: the chance `ambushChance` per 100 blocks across country next to a full camp, less on a road (`ambushByClass`), farther from the camp and for a smaller one, on the land of a kingdom (`ownLandFactor`), near squads holding or guarding (`garrisonFactor`) and patrols (`patrolFactor`) within `protectRadius`. The guards of the caravan fight the robbers by the model of battles (wounded and fallen as in the army). Without guards or if they lose, the caravan loses a share of its goods (`unguardedLoot` or the winner's share), the goods go into the camp and the payment for them goes back to the buyer at once; a merchant of the seller may die (the chronicle, "killed by robbers"). Near players the ambush is real: robbers fall upon the merchant and, if they kill it, take their share. The old random `caravanLossChance` is no longer used: without camps nearby no caravan is lost.
- **Protection.** Squads ordered to hold, guard or patrol (a road too: "Send a squad to patrol it") lower the chance of ambushes and fight a camp within 48 blocks once a day; when they clear it, its goods go to the storehouses of their kingdom. The guild: hunts of `kingdomworld:bandit` and bounties on `kingdomworld:bandit_leader` work as for any monster. Players clear camps themselves and take the chest. Kingdoms learn of a camp when it robs their caravan, a player of theirs comes near it or a squad of theirs fights it.
- **The level of a city:** a village, a town or a large city, by its citizens (a home or the last place within 64 blocks, or everything inside the wall of a city whose wall is wider than 64 blocks) and the buildings around it. A town takes `townPeople` citizens and `townBuildings` buildings, a large city `cityPeople` and `cityBuildings`. The level shows in the Roads tab (the cities view) and on the atlas (one to three gold marks by the sign of a city or the crown of a capital).
- **Walls.** In the Roads tab, the cities view, the Actions button: a palisade (logs of the tag `kingdomworld:wall_wood`, 4 high) or a stone wall with battlements (stone of `kingdomworld:wall_stone`, 5 and 6 high). The wall is laid out as a square ring around the city on the lay of the land (radius `wallRadius` for a village, half as much again for a town, twice for a large city; a capital of districts of a kingdom of the world takes its own radius from its plan, stage 14), leaves out water, dangerous places and the land of others, and is built like a road: builders pay for it column by column from the storehouses, a column stands once its place is loaded, and nothing is broken for it. Where a road crosses the wall a gate opens by itself (an opening three blocks wide); more gates are cut where the king stands ("Cut a gate where I stand"), taking away only blocks of the wall itself, which go to the storehouse. A finished wall and its gates enter the register of buildings as fortifications with the role of a wall (`wall`, category `city_wall`; `kingdomworld:city_wall` of type `fortification` and `kingdomworld:city_gate` of type `gate`) and count for the fortification of the kingdom; the integrity of the wall is counted again every minute while its place is loaded. Upgrades, repairs, demolitions and automatic repairs by template from the Construction tab are off for them with a hint: the wall is kept in the Roads tab, and a wall put up again only adds what is missing (blocks that stand stay, and their payment goes back to the storehouse). A new wall replaces the old one; what was paid and not placed goes back to the storehouse; a wall ordered again is never narrower than the one standing. The atlas draws a wall as a ring (dashed while it is built) with its gates.
- **For later stages:** `CityWalls.level(server, kingdom, city)` (the level, city 0 meaning the capital), `CityWalls.gates(...)` (gates with their places) and the Forge event `CityEntryEvent` (a player came into a city; `throughGate()` tells whether through a gate or past the gates).
- **The Roads tab** of the throne screen: overview, cities (level, wall; actions: palisade, stone wall, gate), roads (class, length, how much is built, condition, who builds, materials still needed; actions: repair, pause, patrol, give up), requests to cross land (allow or refuse), known camps, risks on the ways of deals (the chance of a robbery with the guards, the share of the way on roads, the time on the way). **Rights:** roads and cities need the "Construction" right (`manage_construction`), patrols the "Army" right; no new right and no migration.
- **Groundwork for wars:** a robbery is fought by the `BANDITS` side of the model of battles, and the same mechanics will rob enemy caravans.

### Ancient kingdoms (kingdoms of the world)

- **From the first day (stage 13).** As soon as a place is in the plan (a minute or two after a new world is created; the plan is made a part a tick without generating chunks; in an older world the places that waited for a player are founded right after the update), its kingdom is founded as data: the place of the throne and the capital, land of `landRadius` chunks, names, a ruler, laws and the money of the treasury. The land is protected from that moment, and the atlas and map mods show the borders, the capital, the towns to come and the roads (dashed while not built) at once. Before the founding the land is looked at in the data and the region files: land of others nearby or a chunk where players spent more than `maxInhabitedMinutes` minutes leaves the place alone (as in 8a); no chunk is loaded or generated. While its buildings do not stand, the ruler only grows its land a little (`growthChunks` chunks a turn up to `maxChunks`, again by the region files) and takes no part in diplomacy.
- **Buildings in the world** go up when the place is loaded (`buildMode`). By default `background`: one kingdom at a time with a small budget (`backgroundBlocksPerTick`, `backgroundChunksPerTick`) while players are on the server and the average tick is not longer than `backgroundPauseTickMillis` ms; a kingdom a player comes near (within `triggerChunks` chunks) goes first at the full budget, so the player sees its capital rise. The `approach` mode builds only when a player comes near, as before, but the kingdom is on the map, holds its land and lives already. Money, stores and laws are not given a second time when the buildings rise.
- **The plan.** This is how a world with `plan = "legacy"` and every world of an earlier version plans; a new world lays out states with provinces by default (see "Large states"). When a world first starts (and an older world first starts with this version), the server plans from the seed of the world the places of the kingdoms of the world in the overworld: `count` places between `minSpawnDistance` and `radius` blocks from the spawn, capitals at least `minDistance` apart. A place is judged by the noise of the generator without generating chunks, a candidate a tick: not the ocean, a river, a beach, mountains or peaks, a swamp or mushroom fields, and level ground around the throne. Such a world gets exactly 3 large and 5 small kingdoms (stage 14): `sizeQuota` ([5, 0, 3], small, medium, large) is how many kingdoms of each size the plan makes first, `count` (8) is the total, and kingdoms beyond the quota draw their size by `sizeWeights`. Every place is judged for the largest size still wanted and then for smaller ones: a large capital needs level land under its whole plan. When the candidates run out, the plan goes over them again asking less (rougher ground, a brook allowed, capitals at three quarters of `minDistance`), and on the last pass a smaller kingdom takes the place of a larger one, so a world still gets its 8; all of it is written to the log. Large kingdoms are founded as data and raised in the background first. Places for towns are found `townDistance` blocks around the capital. In an older world a place whose chunks were generated already (read from the region files off the main thread) or that lies near the land of a kingdom is shifted 320 blocks around itself, and given up otherwise. The plan only writes records and leaves the world alone; the Nether, the End and the dimensions of mods take no part.
- **Appearing in the world.** The buildings of a kingdom appear in the world (in the background or when a player comes within `triggerChunks` chunks, see above; not when its chunks are generated, so flying over with elytra does not lag), one kingdom at a time, in stages over ticks with a budget of `blocksPerTick` blocks and `chunksPerTick` chunks. Its chunks are kept loaded with tickets of its own (new ones are generated by the game off the main thread) and let go at the end. The place remembers the stage and the step, and every step looks at the world and the data first, so a restart in the middle goes on where it stopped without copies. Before founding, the place is looked at again: no land of others nearby, and players spent no more than `maxInhabitedMinutes` minutes in its chunks (by the game's count), or the kingdom does not appear. The square of every town and the way to it are looked at the same way, chunk by chunk (in the plan also by the region files: chunks generated already); a town that fails is left out with its road, and its land is neither claimed nor levelled. The throne is placed only after it is clear the kingdom may be founded and nothing that must not be broken stands there.
- **A capital of districts** (stage 14; small, medium, large; kept by places planned with it, while places of the plan of capitals planned since stage 14d get a styled capital, see "Architectural styles and styled capitals"; the capitals of states are capitals of districts, see "Large states"). The throne stands in a palace in the middle of the capital: a great hall for a small or medium kingdom, a castle 35x35 with a courtyard, corner towers and a gatehouse for a large one, the throne inside. A square lies around the palace, and two avenues run from it to gates in the middle of the sides of the wall (a small capital has 2 gates, north and south, a medium or large one 4). Buildings stand in rows along square streets with their fronts on the street, buildings of different widths set apart, and the plan is mirrored by the seed. Districts: the market on the south, crafts and stores on the east, the garrison next to the palace on the north, houses on the west; a way for the guards runs along the wall inside it, gardens lie behind the houses, greens with a tree at open corners, lamp posts along the avenues. A small kingdom: a wall of radius 44 (a palisade), about 23 buildings, a village; a medium one: 54, about 37 buildings, a town; a large one: 82 (a stone wall), about 90 buildings, a large city. The radius of the wall is the capital's own, not `roads.wallRadius`. Corner towers (2 for a small capital, 4 otherwise) and gatehouses (medium and large) stand inside the wall. A small capital has its fields inside the wall on the east and west sides, larger ones outside; the fields are carrots, every third one wheat for the bakers. The capital holds `landRadius` chunks around the throne (a capital of districts takes at least 4, 5 and 7 chunks), its ground is levelled without touching the land of others, and its roads start at its gates.
- **Towns of a kingdom.** Around the capital lie `towns` towns `townDistance` blocks away: two rows along a street with a square and the town hall, 7 buildings for a small or medium kingdom, 9-10 for a large one (houses, a well, a workshop, a wooden watchtower, and for a large kingdom a barracks), a field of carrots, and a palisade around the towns of a large kingdom; the land of a town is 5x5 chunks. Each is reached by a road (a path, gravel or paved by size), and the land between them belongs to the kingdom.
- **Looks.** A kingdom of the world laid out on the grid or in districts (a styled capital has one of the eight styles instead) gets one of five looks by the climate of its land and its number in the plan: stone with the wood of the place, red brick with spruce, sandstone with acacia, deepslate bricks with dark oak, mud bricks with jungle wood; the wall, the paving and every building follow it. Every role has several templates (small houses 4, large houses 4, workshops 5, stores 3, barracks 2, towers 3, wells 2, taverns 2 plus the upgrade, markets 2), chosen by the seed, so a street does not repeat one building.
- **Garrison.** Barracks keep room for the garrison of a later stage: 16 bunks for a small kingdom, 28 for a medium one, 52 for a large one in the capital and 4 in every town of a large kingdom; the number of soldiers (`soldiers`) is unchanged. The plan of the capital holds named guard posts: `gate_north`, `gate_east`, `gate_south`, `gate_west`, `square`, `palace_door`, `palace_hall`, `stores`, `stores_round`, `wall_round`. A ram breaks every stone of the tag `kingdomworld:wall_stone`, and a siege of a wide capital counts the squads at its gates.
- **Life.** There are as many citizens as beds; each comes to a home of its own and works near it. Work posts on the square by the palace (a builder, a carrier, a registrar at the guild desk, a minter at the mint, with a bank beside them), in the workshops, at the market and in the mage tower, fields with composters by the need for food, a grove of saplings at the lumberjack's post and a miner's post outside the wall. Everything goes by the role of a building, not by the name of its template: beds, the profession of a post inside a building (a workshop with an anvil or a blast furnace is a smithy, with a smoker a bakery) and the templates of a role a ruler builds by turns. Soldiers (`soldiers`) come from citizens without work, warriors and archers by turns, with gear from the storehouses; squads ordered to guard stand at the gates of the capital and at the town halls of the towns (without gates or towns one squad guards the capital). A currency of its own, a mint, a market open to outsiders that takes foreign money, prices set automatically.
- **Wealth at the start** comes from nothing only when a kingdom appears, like the chests of villages: money in the treasury (`startingMoney`) journaled as "Founding", food for `startingFoodDays` days for every citizen, `startingMaterials` stacks of logs, planks and cobblestone, iron, seeds and the gear of the soldiers in the storehouses. A place that appears again gets nothing twice. Afterwards the kingdom lives by the usual rules.
- **Laws** are set by the ruler when the kingdom appears, through the law as a king sets them: a fee to enter large cities (higher under a merchant, lower under a just ruler, 20 otherwise), the multiplier of fines (a warrior is harder, a just or generous ruler softer, a proud one harder), the reward for the head of a wanted player (a warrior pays more, a thrifty ruler less, a generous one more) and a ban on slavery under a ruler with the trait of justice.
- **The ruler** has no body and the throne stays empty: a name (in both alphabets, like citizens), king or queen, a leaning (trade, war, building, magic; magic only for a large kingdom) and one or two traits. Names of kingdoms and towns are made of roots and endings in both alphabets and never repeat in a world; the register and commands use the Latin form, and players (the overview, the atlas, the Roads tab, the map mods, the window of a gate, messages) see the name in the language of their client. The screens call them ancient kingdoms: "Ancient kingdom: medium", with the hint that it arose together with the world and no player rules it. The color is the one of the palette other kingdoms use least. The decisions of rulers (taxes, building, hiring, trade, land, roads, laws, answers to robbers) come later; the daily turn of a ruler is already kept.
- **For players:** a kingdom of the world shows on the atlas and on the map mods; the Overview tab and the card of the atlas show its ruler, size and leaning. The "Kingdoms" list on the map tells large kingdoms from small ones, and its hover shows how many citizens and soldiers to expect while the capital is being built (afterwards, how many there are). Its market trades, its guild gives contracts, caravans go along the roads; its land is protected like the land of players. Its ruler takes everyone who asks to be a subject (`/kingdom join`); its throne cannot be taken (only by war, in stage 9), and `admin setruler` refuses it.
- **Rulers (stage 8b).** Every `turnEveryDays` days a ruler looks at a snapshot of its kingdom (citizens and beds, food, stores, surplus and shortages, the treasury and income, buildings and their integrity, the army, camps of robbers near its roads, the wanted, deals, neighbours) and makes up to `decisionsPerTurn` decisions, the most pressing first; answers to deals and to roads over its land come at once and do not count. It plays by the rules of players: money only from taxes, trade and the market, buildings from the materials of its storehouses, workshops and purchases, built by real builders (abstractly far from players, as for players; paid blocks are placed when the ruler briefly loads the place). A house when the beds are nearly taken; a storehouse when the stores are full; buildings by the `roles` table (role=village,town,large city; roles the server does not know are skipped); a place is found on its own level land by the streets without tearing down anything of others, and when there is none the ruler claims free chunks next to its land (never land of others or where players lived) up to `maxChunks`. A kingdom founds a new town with a town hall and a road for every `townPopulation` citizens beyond the towns it has, up to `maxTowns`. Taxes by its traits (a greedy ruler higher, a just or generous one lower), wages, orders for the workshops (bread, weapons, tools), a minter for an idle mint, surplus to the market and abroad, shortages bought, deals with neighbours at market prices with a markup by character. An army by income and threats (a warrior keeps more), patrols on the roads, guards for caravans, a guild reward on the robbers of the nearest camp, roads to neighbours (over the land of a ruler of the world by its character, merchants gladly; over the land of players with the usual request). A poor ruler lets soldiers go, stops building, raises taxes and sells its surplus. When its citizens are unhappy and the treasury can pay, it holds a feast of the life of towns. A mage researches in the tower. A subject with a reputation of 50 or more may be appointed treasurer. Relations to neighbours (a number and the reasons: trade, refusals, disputes over land, passage of roads) are kept for the diplomacy of stage 9.
- **What players see.** Important decrees go to the chronicle (the Citizens tab, with the deaths) as one line, for example "Queen Dobroslava ordered a tavern built", no more than `decreesPerDay` a day, and to the subjects in chat. The Overview tab of a kingdom of the world shows the last decree (hover for all recent ones), what the kingdom needs and saves for (how a player can help or earn) and the attitude of its ruler to the player (from the reputation under its law and deals).
- **The guilds of the kingdoms of the world** (stage 14). The branch of the adventurers' guild and the guild of mages of a kingdom of the world get their ranks at its founding, by the level and the seed of the place: a small one B or A, a medium one A or S, a large one S (`worldRankMin`, `worldRankMax`), and rank SS belongs to one kingdom of the world, the strongest, the large one founded first while SS is free. The guild the ruler cares for keeps that rank and the other stands a rank lower (not below the lowest rank of the level): under a warrior the adventurers stand higher, under a mage the mages. When the capital is raised, adventurers come from abroad (`worldAdventurers`: 5, 8, 11, 14 for B, A, S, SS; the head of the rank of the branch, two or three a rank lower, the others two and three lower, none below D) and mages for the posts of the mage tower (`worldMages`: 3, 5, 7, 10), and the guild of mages knows its first researches (`worldResearch`: 3, 5, 7, 8) and holds energy (`worldEnergy`: 1000, 2000, 3000, 4000) at once. Every styled capital has a mage tower (of the rank of its guilds); of the capitals of districts of older worlds only a large one has it, and the small and medium ones get no mages, though their guild of mages has its rank, researches and energy. Adventurers and mages live as citizens: they eat from the storehouses and, while they have no bed of their own, lodge at the guild (adventurers) and in the tower (mages) as the army does in its tents, and take a bed when one is free or the ruler builds a house; an adventurer has no wage, a mage the usual one (the tax brings most of it back to the treasury). Every day the adventurers hunt far from players and bring the energy of crystals home to the guild of mages (for each of them the crystal of a monster a rank below with its chance, about 90 a day at B and 16500 at SS, up to the capacity of the store); the guild keeps the ward over the throne and the chunks around it (3x3) while there is energy for three days of it, and switches healing on. A ruler of any leaning researches further (a mage more readily), keeping the energy for three days of the ward. Every `worldGrowthDays` (40) days a kingdom whose treasury holds at least what it was founded with raises each guild a rank, up to the highest rank of its level (SS for the strongest alone). Kingdoms of the world founded before get their ranks when next looked at (once a minute), and their adventurers and mages come little by little, no more than four a game day (the fallen are replaced the same way). The ranks show in the Kingdoms list of the map (the rank of the stronger guild beside the size, both on hover) and on the card of the kingdom. The energy of the hunts goes to the guild of mages of the kingdom of the world alone: players get no money or items from nothing.
- **Older worlds.** The plan is made once, so the quota applies to new worlds. Places planned or founded before keep the old grid layout and are raised by the old code; kingdoms already standing are not rebuilt. Data format of the kingdoms of the world 4 (was 3), network protocol 26.

### Architectural styles and styled capitals

Stage 14d gives every capital of a kingdom of the world a castle, buildings of its institutions and houses that differ from one another, all in one of eight architectural styles; kingdoms of players choose a style too. Every place of the plan of capitals (`plan = "legacy"`) planned from this stage on gets a styled capital; the capitals of the states a new world lays out by default are capitals of districts (see "Large states").

- **Eight styles.** A style sets shapes, not only colors: the outline of roofs and towers, windows, arches, ornament, materials and furniture. The wall of a capital, its squares and its streets follow it too (the palisade of a small capital is of the wood of its style).

  | Style | What it looks like | Stone wall of the capital |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `classic` | stone and half-timbering under slate roofs, round towers under cones | stone bricks |
  | `terem` | log frames with carved porches, tented roofs and onion domes, a kremlin of white stone | polished diorite |
  | `nordic` | dark tarred staves, steep shingle roofs, dragon heads on the ridges | cobblestone |
  | `southern` | whitewashed walls, red tiled roofs, arcades and bell towers | smooth sandstone |
  | `desert` | sandstone and mud brick, flat roofs, domes and slender minarets | mud bricks |
  | `eastern` | dark timber and white plaster under sweeping curved roofs, pagodas | stone |
  | `gothic` | dark slate, pointed windows, buttresses and needle spires | deepslate bricks |
  | `mountain` | a hold of heavy stone grown into the rock, with forges and the faces of forefathers | cobbled deepslate |

- **The style of a kingdom of the world** comes from the climate of its land (cold: `nordic` first; dry: `desert`; lush: `eastern`; temperate: `classic`; mountains or high land around the throne put `mountain` first), and no two kingdoms of a world share one: a place takes the first style of its climate's list that no other planned or standing kingdom of the world holds, so styles repeat only beyond eight kingdoms. The style is kept with the place and never changes.
- **The castle** stands around the throne in the middle of every capital, a plan of its own for every style and size: small 31x31, medium 41x41, large 47x47, 40-48 blocks high with its spires. A curtain wall with battlements and a wall walk, corner towers, a gatehouse, a courtyard with a garden, a well or a fountain, and a keep high above the town with the throne hall, banners in the color of the kingdom, the royal chambers, the kitchen and the council room; the throne stands in the hall of the keep, and its doors open to everybody. A large capital also has the royal guard barracks (16 bunks), stables and a garden in the ring by the castle.
- **Institutions.** A square lies around the castle, and the institutions face it:
  - the guild house of the adventurers: a hall with the contract board, the guild desk and the registrar;
  - the mage tower, the seat of the guild of mages, with the magic focus, a library, a brewing room and two mages; every capital has one, a landmark on the square;
  - the treasury with the mint, the minter and the bank (the server places the bank block where the template marks it);
  - the court with a guardroom and cells: its barracks block makes it a place to buy confiscated goods back, like the throne, a town hall and a barracks;
  - the infirmary, where the citizens and soldiers of the capital are cared for;
  - a tavern of the style;
  - in the outer ring the builders' yard and the carters' yard with their builders and carriers (a medium or large capital keeps more of them at posts by its gates);
  - at the corners of the square 3 (small) or 4 decorations without a role by the seed: a monument, a chapel, a clock tower, a park.

  The treasury, the court, the infirmary, the yards and the decorations have the classic shape in every style, built of the materials and colors of the style.
- **Guild houses and mage towers by rank.** The guild house and the mage tower come in four sizes for the ranks B, A, S and SS of the guild: the higher the rank, the larger, taller and richer (B about 13x15, SS 31-35 by 33-37 and one of the main buildings of the capital after the castle; a mage tower of B is 11-13 wide and 27-33 high, of SS 25-27 wide and 45-48 high). Until the guilds rank themselves in play (stage 14e), the rank comes from the size of the kingdom (`GuildHouses.rankOf`): a small kingdom has B or A, a medium one A or S by its seed, a large one S, and the first large kingdom planned in a world the only SS. The rank is decided once when the place is planned and kept with it (the optional key `guild` of the place), and the guild house and the mage tower of a capital share it. The catalog names the rank of every such template (`TemplateCatalog.guildHouse(style, kind, rank)`).
- **Quarters.** Two rings of buildings stand between the square and the wall. The inner ring is the noble quarter: manors and merchants' houses among the institutions, their fronts on the square. In the outer ring, along every row from the avenue to the corner: the market on the south side (with a fountain and a market hall in a medium or large capital), the workshops, stores and the barracks and store of the guard; then the houses: merchants' by the market on the south, craftsmen's on the east and west, the poor along the north wall behind the castle and in the far halves of the east and west rows towards the north corners. Houses go up by turns on every side, the poor rows first, up to a third of the homes left, so that no side stays empty when the capital has homes enough; the room left stays as lots for buildings to come. In the deep gardens behind the buildings trees grow in rows. Two avenues (four in a medium or large capital) run from the square to the wall gates of the style, with corner towers of the style at the corners of the wall.
- **Rows that are not a line.** Every house stands 0-2 blocks back from its street, the gaps between buildings differ, a house set two blocks back gets a front garden with a fence of its wood and flowers, trees grow in wide gaps, in deep back gardens and at the free corner of the square, lamps stand along the avenues; no two equal templates stand side by side.
- **Looks.** Every building takes its look from its style, its quarter and the seed of its plot: roof, plaster, cloth, rough and carved stone, trim, shutters and chimney from 3-4 variants of the style, so neighbouring houses of one template differ, and small details change with the seed (a chimney or none, the kind of awning, the shutters). The castle, the guild, the mage tower and the public buildings carry banners and carpets in the color of the kingdom. The look is fixed when a building is laid out and kept with the construction, so a restart in the middle of an appearance goes on with the same buildings.
- **Size.**

  | | small | medium | large |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | radius of the wall | 74 (a palisade, 2 gates) | 86 (a stone wall, 4 gates) | 101 (a stone wall, 4 gates) |
  | land of the capital, at least | 6 chunks (13x13) | 7 (15x15) | 8 (17x17) |
  | buildings in the capital, without towns | 38-46 | 53-63 | 69-82 |
  | rank of the guild and the mage tower | B or A | A or S | S, the first one SS |
  | bunks in the capital | 16 | 28 | 56 |

  A small capital has its fields inside the wall, larger ones outside. Towns lie farther out, beyond the wider land of the capital.
- **Towns and camps in style.** A town of a styled kingdom has houses of its style no larger than 11x11, the smithy of its style or a workshop, a well and the watchtower of its style; a town of a large kingdom also has a tavern and a barracks. The camps of the royal army of a styled kingdom are built in its style (a drill yard, two barracks, a store and a tower: `<style>_camp_yard`, `<style>_camp_barracks`, `<style>_camp_store`, `<style>_camp_tower`) with the look of its public buildings; kingdoms of the older layouts keep the plain camp.
- **Workers and posts.** The registrar works at the guild desk inside the guild house, the minter at the mint inside the treasury, two mages in the mage tower, builders and carriers in their yards; every trade of the plan gets at least one worker, and professions still go by the blocks of a building, not by its name. The plan keeps the guard posts of stage 14 (`gate_*`, `square`, `palace_door`, `palace_hall` in the throne hall, `stores`, `stores_round`, `wall_round`) and adds `castle_gate`, `castle_wall` (a round on the wall walk of the castle) and `treasury`. The garrison fills these three after the others, so a small capital rarely has guards left for them.
- **Rulers.** A ruler of a styled kingdom builds the templates of its style and those of no style (its houses, smithy and tavern always in its style), each laid with the look of its quarter, and its builder waits on the square before the castle. Rulers of capitals of the older layouts build the plain templates as before.
- **Kingdoms of players.**
  - The founding screen shows the eight styles with a picture (the large castle of the style), the name and, on hover, the caption. The style the climate and the mountains around the throne suggest is chosen and marked "Suits the land"; the arrows choose another. Styles of players need not be unique: several kingdoms of players and a kingdom of the world may share one.
  - The style is kept with the kingdom. A kingdom founded before styles existed takes the one its land suggests the first time it is asked and keeps it.
  - The Overview tab of the throne shows the style of every kingdom. The king of a kingdom of players changes it there: "Change style" opens the styles on the current one, "Build in this style" names the fee and asks to confirm. The treasury pays `styleChangeCost` (`[kingdom]`, 2000 copper coins by default, 0 makes it free), journaled as "Admin"; the same style or a treasury that cannot pay is refused. New buildings follow the new style, those standing are not rebuilt. A kingdom of the world keeps the style of its place.
  - The Construction catalog shows the templates of the kingdom's style first, marked with a gold bar, then those of no style and then the other styles; the filter button beside the mode button goes through all styles, the own style and each of the eight. A king may build in any style.
  - The walls of a kingdom of players are built of the stone of its style, or of the logs of its wood for a palisade, while the storehouses hold enough of it, otherwise of the most plentiful stone of the tag as before. Gate towers and corner towers are not added to the walls of players by themselves; the wall gates and towers of the styles are in the catalog.
- **Older worlds and data.** Places planned or founded before keep their layout (the grid or the capital of districts) and the plain templates, and kingdoms already standing are not rebuilt; only places of the plan of capitals planned from now on are styled. A place keeps its style in `style` and its guild rank in the optional key `guild`, a kingdom its style in the optional key `style`; no format number changed with it (kingdoms 9, kingdoms of the world 5 then). An older build of the mod keeps the data of the kingdoms of the world read-only when it meets a styled capital: going back to a build without styles is not supported. Network protocol 33.
- **Upgrading a server with its config.** Forge keeps the values of an existing config, so a server that ran an older version keeps `npcKingdoms.rulers.maxChunks` = [96, 160, 256]: less than the land a styled capital starts with (169, 225, 289 chunks), and its ruler then claims no land and founds no towns. Set the new defaults [224, 352, 640] (or more) in `<world>/serverconfig/kingdomworld-server.toml`.

### Large states

Stage 15 (version 1.4.0) makes the kingdoms of the world states. The wide land of each is split into provinces, large cities, small towns, villages and hamlets stand in them with roads between them, and the land where a state does not live stays wild. Provinces are sold, ceded by treaty and taken in war, province by province. A new world lays out states by default; a world whose plan is made or begun already (every world of an earlier version among them) keeps its plan of capitals, told of in "Ancient kingdoms". The files and formats of the data are in "Data and data safety".

- **The plan of states.** The key `plan` in `[npcKingdoms]`: `states` (the default) lays out states, `legacy` places capitals as before. The key acts only on a new world, whose plan is chosen in its first tick: a plan made is never made again, one begun goes on, and a world that lived more than a minute before its plan began (every world of 1.3.0 and older, a world whose kingdoms of the world were switched on later) keeps its capitals whatever the key says. For capitals in a new world put `plan = "legacy"` under `[npcKingdoms]` in `defaultconfigs/kingdomworld-server.toml` before the world is made (Forge copies that file into a new world); the key in the config of a world already started changes nothing, its plan was chosen on its first start. A new world gets 3 large and 5 small states (`sizeQuota` [5, 0, 3], the land of each within `stateChunks`) within `radius` of the spawn (7000 blocks; the plan takes 20000 at most), no closer than `spawnZone` (1500 blocks) to it, with a neutral strip at least `gapBlocks` (384 blocks) wide between states and at least `neutralShare` (35%) of the land neutral. The server goes over the land in cells of 64 blocks within `radius` + 512 blocks: 4 probes of the biome and the height of the ground from the noise of the generator, without generating chunks (the height is guessed from the depth of the noise, and a column of the noise is worked out only where the guess lies within 8 blocks of the sea level and decides between water and land); the survey is kept in `kingdomworld_survey.dat`. Then the server marks the chunks generated already (by the headers of the region files, off the main thread; a region whose file cannot be read counts as generated all over) and the land of kingdoms with 4 chunks around it, lays out the states and the provinces on a thread of its own, judges the thrones in the world (a throne never stands on water or steep ground), draws the borders along the water chunk by chunk, writes the provinces into the map of the land up to 32 tiles a tick, and founds the states as data one a tick, each given its provinces at once; the capitals are raised in the background as those of every kingdom of the world are. The looks at the noise (the survey, the thrones, the borders) run on the three noise threads of the plan of the kingdoms of the world, and the server only puts their answers in order in its ticks: the plan takes 8 ms a tick at most (in fact a fraction of a millisecond), its stage is kept in the file of the provinces, and every stage goes on from its place after a restart, to the same outcome. The plan of a new world is ready in less than a minute after the start (the measures are in "Performance"). `/kingdomworld admin land plan` shows the stage, the work done and the time of every stage, `admin land survey` what the survey found; at the end the log gets the outcome: the sizes of the states, the number of provinces, the neutral share, the time.
- **The capital of a state** is a capital of districts (see "Ancient kingdoms"): a palace around the throne, a castle of 35x35 in a large state, the districts of the market, the crafts, the garrison and the houses in one of five looks by the climate of the land. A capital of a state has no castle in one of the eight styles, no guild house and no mage tower by rank (a mage tower stands only in a large one): those belong to the styled capitals of the plan `legacy` (see "Architectural styles and styled capitals").
- **Provinces.** The land of a state is split into provinces of about `provinceChunks` (1600) chunks: the heart (the province of the capital), the provinces of the core with room for towns, and the borderlands the plan leaves empty; the neutral land between states is split into wild provinces of nobody. A province protects the land of its holder as a claim does, but a claim always wins; the chunk limit counts only the claims, while the throne counts the land with the provinces (see "Territory and protection"). A province changes hands as a whole, in one event: by the plan, a purchase, a treaty of cession, a peace or an annexation; when its kingdom is dissolved it becomes nobody's. Operator commands: `admin province fill|give|info|clear` and `admin land info|unlock`.
- **Settlements.** After the provinces the plan places the settlements of every state: the capital at the throne, large cities, small towns, villages and hamlets (a large state has 4-8 large cities, 10-15 small towns, 30-40 villages and hamlets). Every place is judged by the noise of the generator on the noise threads of the plan: flat dry land, not on chunks generated already and not on the land of kingdoms.
