When a place does not suit, up to three alternates are tried, then places near it in the same province (two rings at 48 and 96 blocks), and such a place keeps its distance from every other settlement; a settlement no place suits is left out with a line in the log. When a state is founded its settlements are written as data: rank, place, province, the settlement it belongs to, a name no other in the world has, houses by the seed (a large city 80-150, a small town 25-45, a village 10-25, a hamlet 4-8) with three quarters of them taken by people, food for 10 days, a guard (8 in a large city, 4 in a small town), the militia of a village (a tenth of its people), prosperity and the yield of the land from the cells of the survey around it; the main settlement of a province becomes its seat. Once a game day the settlements live their day in numbers, 16 a tick: fed people grow by 2% a day up to the room of their houses, food is the harvest less what is eaten with a store of 30 days at most, and hunger takes people and prosperity. The numbers make no coins and no items: treasuries and storehouses live by the named citizens alone.
- **Settlements in the world.** Settlements rise in the world one at a time, in the one slot they share with the capitals: while a capital rises, a settlement waits. A settlement holds its land and a ring of a chunk with a ticket of its own (a large city 13x13 = 169 chunks, a small town 7x7 = 49, a village 5x5 = 25, a hamlet 4x4 = 16), let go once it stands or is put aside. Its stages: loading; a look at its land (the land of another kingdom, a chunk where players spent longer than `maxInhabitedMinutes`, what may not be broken; when it does not suit, the place moves once by one to three chunks within its province, otherwise the settlement stays data, its people go as a number to the nearest settlement of its kingdom, and a province whose main settlement was put aside so is held in war as an empty one); the ground; the town hall and the buildings; the fields; the beds and posts of its own land (not of the whole kingdom) with professions by what the buildings are for; its founding stores once (bread for its people for `startingFoodDays` days, wood and stone by its rank); its citizens; its guard; its wall; its posts. The stage and the step are kept in its record, and a restart goes on from there without copies of buildings or citizens. The buildings take the look of their kingdom, as its capital does: a small town is a town of its kingdom (two rows along a street by the town hall), a village four to six of its plots without a tower, a hamlet two or three houses by a well, and a large city the districts of a medium capital with a town hall for a palace, 40 buildings at most on land of 11 by 11 chunks, with a wall and gates; the small towns of a large state get a palisade. A player closer than `settlementTriggerChunks` (a large city 16, a small town 14, a village 12, a hamlet 10 chunks) raises a settlement with the full budget and puts aside a capital nobody watches; once every capital stands the background raises the large cities and small towns (`backgroundTiers`) within `backgroundSettlementBlocks` (2000) blocks of players, and villages and hamlets rise only when a player comes. The named citizens of a settlement arrive 4 a tick, as many as its people and beds allow, each always the same person for its slot (id, name, look and age from the seed of the settlement and the slot). The guard of a settlement (8 and 4) comes from abroad into the guard of the kingdom with kits paid by its treasury, without taking citizens from their work, and stands at the town hall; villages keep their militia as a number only. A settlement the journal of conquests hands over rises no more for the loser, and one handed over while it rises goes on for the winner from what stands (its ground levelled again or its places looked at again, never a second layout over the first); a settlement a kingdom of players won does not rise. A large state with all its settlements stays within the limits of a kingdom of the world (354 soldiers and 47 squads at most). Commands `admin npc settlement info|raise <id>`.
- **The citizens of settlements as numbers.** The holders of the posts of a standing town stay named while it stands. The other citizens (and all of a village or a hamlet) stay named while a player was near (the activation of bodies and 4 chunks more) in the last `settlerKeepDays` game days; then those nothing refers to fold into the numbers of their settlement, 16 a tick, 8 bytes each (slot, profession, age, mood), their things going to the storehouses of their kingdom. When a player comes they come back as themselves (the same id, name, look, age and profession), 4 a tick, into their beds and with nothing in their hands. Citizens with a body, soldiers and members of squads, adventurers, hirelings and those away on an expedition of the guild, the sick and wounded and those holding a reservation of a workshop never fold. Above `maxNamedCitizens` (5000 in the world) newcomers and returns wait and the settlements no player visited longest fold first. The limit `maxCitizensPerKingdom` of a kingdom with settlements counts its capital only, the settlements are bounded by their beds, and their free beds wait for their citizens. A settlement in the journal of conquests neither folds nor brings back citizens until it is the winner's; the citizens of a settlement of a kingdom of players never fold, and those folded before come back as themselves. A death in a settlement is a citizen less in its numbers. The card of a settlement and the Citizens tab tell how many of its people are named. Commands `admin npc settlement fold|unfold <id>`.
- **The roads of the plan.** The plan lays roads between the places of the settlements it took. A road of the plan becomes a real road when both settlements stand (a raised settlement or the capital of a state that stands) and a player is within 12 chunks of it, and without a player one in two minutes in the background; when a settlement rises and the settlement it belongs to stands already, the road between them is searched for at once. The search goes along the line of the plan in a corridor of 64 blocks, in stretches of 256 blocks at most and 30 thousand steps each (players keep their limit of 400 thousand), the stretches joined into one road and a point that cannot be reached moved aside; one such road at a time, among the four searches and one a kingdom. The road is paid once, as the roads of the founding are, and laid as its chunks are loaded; one not found waits a day and says so in the log. Caravans go along the roads of the plan not laid yet as along tracks, 1.2 times as fast as across country. The limits `maxCities`, `minCityDistance` and `maxRoads` are for kingdoms of players; a kingdom of the world has `[roads] npcMaxRoads` (200). The way of a road is kept a byte a block (a road of 1000 blocks takes about 1 KB instead of 16), and the maps draw roads by their bends (no more than 3 blocks off).
- **The wilds.** The land of the provinces of a kingdom of the world where it does not live (no claim, building, wall or road of it) is as free to players as land of nobody: breaking, placing, opening chests, buckets, fire and entities go without a crime or a record of damage, explosions break and fire burns; striking a citizen stays a matter of the law. Where the kingdom lives (its capital and towns, buildings, walls, roads) the rules are as before. A bed, a workbench or a storehouse of a player in the wilds is no home, post or storehouse of the kingdom. A road through the wilds needs no consent and costs no more. The land of kingdoms of players (their bought provinces too) is never wild. Camps of robbers stand in the wilds: at least 400 blocks from the claimed land of kingdoms of the world, `campMinDistance` from the claims of players and 128 blocks from roads and other camps; new configs allow 16 camps (`maxCamps`). Key `[land] wildernessOpen` (on by default).
- **Buying provinces and charters.** A ruler of the world sells an empty province of its edge or core (not its heart, not next to its heart, none with a settlement standing or planned) while it keeps three quarters of its planned provinces and three quarters of its land together with its capital; once in 10 days and a quarter of its planned provinces in all, not to an enemy at war and not to one it thinks worse of than -20. The price: the chunks of the province at `[land] provincePricePerChunk` (3) in the currency of the seller, x0.85 from a merchant, x0.9 from a generous ruler, x1.25 from a thrifty or proud one, x1.4 from a warrior, by the relation from x1.2 (0 and worse) to x0.9 (50 and better), at least 1500: a province of 1600 chunks costs about 3700-8100, a goal rather than a purchase on the way. The ruler answers as it answers a deal: a merchant readily, a warrior seldom, the same all day. A kingdom buys a province next to its land, bought by a member with the Treasury and Territory rights: its treasury pays in its own currency by the rates of the day into the reserves of the seller, a line "Land" in the books of both, and the province is its own at once, not counted in the limit of claims. A player without a kingdom buys a charter: the price goes from the account into escrow (in the currency of the seller or another current one) and the province is held for 3 days; a throne set in it founds the kingdom (the land of this province is no foreign land to it, other land is checked as always), the province is the kingdom's at once and the money goes to the seller. Without a throne in 3 days the money comes back whole. A charter on a province that changes hands by war or by treaty is void, its money back to its bearer. Commands `/kingdom province price [id]` and `/kingdom province buy <id>`; on the atlas the card of a province with its price, a "Buy" button ("Buy a charter" without a kingdom) and the reason it is not for sale on hover, and the line at the bottom says "for sale" with the price.
- **The growth of states.** The states of the world grow by provinces, not by claims: a wild province next to them in 12 days while their land is under 130% of their plan, not near the spawn, not with a claim of a player in it or within 4 chunks, not where players spent more than `maxInhabitedMinutes` (the headers of the region files, then up to 64 chunks read off the server thread, 4 a tick), and while 35% of the land of the provinces stays neutral. A new town of a state is a village in an empty province of its core, and when no such province is left a ruler makes a village not raised yet (far enough from other towns) a small town. Once no wild land beside it is free, a ruler disputes its land with the holders of the provinces next to its own and counts them among its neighbours however far their capitals stand; a warrior goes to war for land on a bordering state of the world twice weaker.
- **War by provinces.** The land of provinces is taken in war by provinces, of any kingdom, the bought provinces of a kingdom of players among them, while claimed chunks are held one by one as before (see "War in the world"). A province where people live (its main settlement stands or waits to rise) is held at the square of that settlement (`plazaMinutes`, 5 minutes), an empty one at the boundary stone in its middle chunk (`occupyMinutes`, 3 minutes; the stone is data, no block). Players hold a province standing in the square or in the chunk of the stone; far from players a squad ordered to hold, guard or besiege within 24 blocks of the place holds it, but first, at most once a minute, fights by numbers the garrison of the settlement (a guard of strength 25, a militiaman of 12) and the squads of the defenders there: the fallen are taken from the guards first, then from the militia, and the guards who serve in the settlement as soldiers fight as their squad and are not counted again in the number of the garrison; while defenders stand the hold is interrupted. A war for land holds any province but the heart (the province of the capital), a war for a city only those where people live; the town of a settlement of a state is held only as the square of its province, and a ruler of the world besieges no such town. A province taken stays taken until the peace, gives score once (twice where people live), goes to the chronicle and the rumors and is told to both sides by its name. A peace for land hands over the provinces taken, the first taken first, as many as the lead allows (full terms 2, halved 1), a peace for a city one province where people live (of the settlement named, else the first taken), and only an annexation takes the heart; the terms of the peace name the provinces handed over. A kingdom of players with bought provinces gives up its chunks and its city by the old rules beside them.
- **The treaty of cession.** In the "Diplomacy" tab a button goes through the provinces of a neighbour that touch the land of the kingdom (never its heart nor one promised by a charter; the name and the price on hover), and the kingdom asks for the one chosen ("Ask to cede") at the price a ruler of the world asks for a province on sale (twice that where people live, nothing when it would give it away anyway), or for nothing from a kingdom of players, which answers among its offers. A ruler answers by its character and relations: never its heart, never to one it thinks worse of than -20, never for nothing when it is a warrior. The price is paid by the books on the line "Land" and the province is the asker's at once, while what stands in it follows by the journal of conquests. A province an enemy holds or has taken, one a claim of a peace or a conquest in the journal names, and one of a kingdom being annexed can be neither asked for nor ceded. A war between the two calls the offer off. A cession goes to the chronicle and the rumors.
- **The journal of conquests.** The journal hands provinces over whole, one event each, then the claims of the loser in them 512 a second, the buildings 64 a second, the citizens 16 a second, and the cities and settlements one a second (with their walls and a new look at the places of their loaded chunks where people live); an annexation takes every province at once, the claims 512 a second and the settlements before the kingdom is dissolved. All steps of the journal share 8 ms a second and find what is left in the world again after a restart, so nothing is lost or doubled.
- **Rulers at war.** A ruler of the world sends its field army to hold the nearest province of the enemy where people live, in a war for land then the boundary stone of the nearest empty one, one at a time and on to the next once it is taken; the defender sends its field army or half of its free squads to relieve it and calls them home at the peace. When the place of a province moved, the army and the relief go on to the new place.
- **On the map.** Players see the provinces of their dimension: when they join or change dimension the server sends their map in parts of 32 tiles a tick (tiles as runs, the table of the provinces and their holders; a world of 600 tiles and 350 provinces weighs about 95 KB, about 36 KB compressed), then only what changes, and a province changing hands is one message however many chunks it has. A province of a kingdom a player may not see reaches that player as a province of nobody. On the kingdom atlas (up to 1280 chunks across) the land of a state is filled with its color, borders between states are thick and between provinces thin, wild land is hatched, the names of provinces show from half a pixel a chunk, and the footer tells the province under the pointer, what it is (heart of the state, province, borderland, wild land), who holds it and its main settlement. Settlements are drawn by rank: a large city shows from 0.4 pixels a chunk, a small town from 1.5, a village from 3, a hamlet from 6, their names closer; until a settlement is raised its sign has a dashed frame, and the roads of the plan are dashed too; a settlement under the pointer shows a card (rank, people, guard and militia, province). A province being held is marked with a diamond and the share held, a taken one with a banner, the boundary stones of the empty provinces of states show as signs, and the line above the hotbar shows the hold of a province. JourneyMap, Xaero's maps, BlueMap and Dynmap draw every province as an area of its own in the color of its holder and show settlements as cities; the web maps show contested land of 3 by 3 chunks around the place of every province held. The cities and roads of the map reach players in parts by kingdom with versions: a player gets again only the parts that changed, 192 KB a packet at most, and the settlements and planned roads of a kingdom hidden from a player do not reach that player. The throne of a kingdom of the world shows how many settlements it has and their people, the Citizens tab lists a row for every settlement after the named citizens, and the ruler sees the numbers of its settlements.
- **Older worlds and upgrading.** A world of an earlier version, any world that lived more than a minute before its plan began, and a world whose plan of capitals is made or begun keep the plan of capitals: no provinces or states appear in them, and their kingdoms of the world live as before. Forge keeps the values of an existing config: the config of a world of an earlier version keeps `radius` 6000 and `maxCamps` 8, and Forge adds to it the key `plan` it did not have with `states`, which changes nothing in such a world; a new world gets the new defaults. New files `kingdomworld_provinces.dat` and `kingdomworld_survey.dat` (format 1), new formats `kingdomworld_npc.dat` 6, `kingdomworld_roads.dat` 2, `kingdomworld_siege.dat` 3 and `kingdomworld_diplomacy.dat` 2; older formats are read as they are, and an older build of the mod that meets a newer format keeps that data read-only. Network protocol 40: client and server must both run version 1.4.0.

### Life of cities
- **The day.** Morning (from `wakeUp` to `workStart`): out of the house and on the way to work; day (until `eveningStart`): work; evening (until `sleepStart`): some citizens (`tavernShare`, others every evening) go to the tavern of their city, the others walk near home; night: they sleep lying in their own bed. Every citizen keeps its day up to `spreadTicks` earlier or later, so a city does not get up and lie down in step. Soldiers and adventurers on duty do not sleep, the innkeeper works through the evening. Far from players the day is counted without bodies: an evening in an open tavern gives mood, sleep at home heals a little.
- **Sleep and the law.** A sleeping citizen is no witness. A theft does not wake it; a blow (assault or murder) closer than 8 blocks does, and the woken citizen sees the offender if it has a line of sight. At night citizens see a theft only from half the usual distance; guards see as by day.
- **The tavern.** The building role `tavern` in the register of 4b (`guests` served an evening: 12, +8 per level) and the built-in templates "Tavern" (a counter with the innkeeper's post, barrels, tables with benches, lanterns, three rooms upstairs) and "Tavern with rooms" (level 2 through `upgrades_to`); wood and stone change like for every template. The innkeeper (`innkeeper`) works at a post inside the tavern; while healthy, the tavern is open. The beds of a tavern are no homes of citizens but rooms for guests; a room is not rented while its bed is somebody's home, the point of return of another player or somebody lies in it. A citizen makes its home only in a bed of a building of the register (a house, a barracks, a tower of mages, a building without a role), and in a bed outside buildings only while the kingdom has no working house yet: this is how a new kingdom gets its first citizens. A bed a player sleeps in as their point of return is nobody's home: a citizen who lived in it looks for another, and a citizen asleep in a bed a player lies down in gets up. Citizens who already live in a bed outside buildings stay. In the evening a citizen in an open tavern eats or drinks one thing from the storehouses (tags `kingdomworld:tavern_drink` and `kingdomworld:tavern_food`: vanilla and optional tags of mods) as its dinner and is in a better mood (`tavernMood` for `tavernMoodDays` days). Without a tavern the mood does not fall, there is just no bonus. Kingdoms of the world (8a) build a tavern with an innkeeper when medium and the tavern with rooms when large.
- **The window of the tavern** (right click the innkeeper): food and drink from the storehouses at the prices of the market (the king's price if the market sells it, otherwise the price of the market by worth and stock; reputation moves it as at the market), the money goes to the treasury (the ledger line "Taverns and festivals"); the rumors of the day; a room for a day for `roomPrice`: a bed of the tavern becomes the point of return, after the day the one before comes back (also for a player who is offline, when they come back). Payment with coins or from the account.
- **Rumors.** The innkeeper tells 2-4 rumors (`rumorsMin`, `rumorsMax`), new ones every game day, one of each kind first. All from the data of the world: camps of robbers closer than 1500 blocks (one of eight points of the compass, the distance in steps of 50 blocks, "a few robbers", "a band" or "a large band"), open guild contracts of the kingdom with their reward, news (new kingdoms of the last 10 days, citizens killed by robbers or fallen in battle, festivals and fairs of other kingdoms), where goods are cheap and where they are dear, whom the guards of this kingdom look for. A rumor of a camp can be bought for `rumorPrice`: the camp appears on the player's atlas. Other stages add their kinds of rumors with `LifeRumors.register(id, RumorSource)` (the wars and weddings of stages 9 and 10).
- **Light illnesses.** A healthy citizen falls ill with a chance of `sickChance` permille a day, `sickFactor` times more often for each of: hungry, homeless, wounded. An ill citizen works slower (`sickWorkPercent`), its mood is lower by `sickMood`, it goes to the infirmary like a wounded one (the places are shared) and gets well in `infirmaryDays` there or in `homeDays` without one. Nobody dies of an illness and nobody catches it. On the game test server illnesses by chance are off; the tests make them themselves. The "Citizens" tab shows who is ill, where they are treated and how far they got.
- **Festivals.** The king or a member with the right `manage_citizens` (the right of the professions of citizens: a festival is a matter of the citizens) calls a festival in the "Citizens" tab, view "life": the treasury pays `festivalPerCitizen` for every citizen, the storehouses give a treat of `festivalFood` food points per citizen (without the treat a festival gives half the mood), at most once in `festivalEveryDays` days. From noon the citizens rest and gather at the throne or the town hall of their city, the mood `festivalMood` holds for `festivalMoodDays` days, the chronicle of festivals records it. Once a year the day the kingdom was founded is a festival by itself (a year is `daysPerYear` = 96 game days, twelve turns of the moon of 8 days), free, with a treat if there is one.
- **Fairs.** Every `fairEveryDays` days every city of level `fairLevel` (large by default) with a working market holds a fair on a day of its own: the merchant posts of the city take `fairSeats` merchants more, the market nearby sells `fairDiscount` permille cheaper, and the merchants of every kingdom that has a deal with the host and a way partly by road bring up to `fairVisitorItems` items of their export surplus from their storehouses and put them up as lots of their kingdom. The takings go to the merchants' treasury (into the reserves of the host's currency), what is left goes back to their storehouses the next day. Members of the kingdom and players on its land get a message.
- **On the atlas** a festival is a flag in the colour of the kingdom over the capital, a fair a striped awning over the city. The "Citizens" tab, view "life": how many citizens sleep, work, sit in the tavern and rest now, the day by the hour, taverns and the guests of the evening, the festival (today, how long ago, when one may be called and what it costs, the button), the fairs of the cities, the ill and the chronicle of festivals and fairs.
- **Load.** No new scans of the world every tick: the goal of the day thinks once a second, finds taverns in the register of buildings and takes paths from the shared budget; festivals, fairs and rooms are looked at every 5 seconds, rumors are made once a game day per tavern.

### Diplomacy and war

- **Relations.** Every pair of kingdoms (of players and of the world) has a relation from -100 to 100 with its reasons (treaties, the alliance, help and refusal in war, broken word, declined offers, war, peace). It cools a little every day. A ruler of the world adds its own memory of the neighbour (deals, roads, land).
- **Bonds.** Peace (the default), a pact of non-aggression for `pactDays` days, a defensive alliance, vassalage (the vassal pays its overlord `vassalPermille` permille of the income of the day before: taxes, trade, the law, taverns; it declares no wars of its own; the overlord joins its wars), war (from the declaration) and a truce of `truceDays` days after a war (no war can be declared, it cannot be broken).
- **Offers** work like deals of trade: a pact, an alliance, vassalage (become a vassal or demand fealty), freedom of a vassal, peace on terms. They wait `proposalMinutes` minutes for an answer. The right `manage_diplomacy` belongs to the king and the chancellor (old worlds get it once, upgrade `diplomacy_rights`). Breaking a pact or an alliance costs the relation with the victim and with everybody who knows either of them (chronicle, rumors). An overlord may let a vassal go; a vassal leaves in peace with consent or by a war for independence.
- **Declaring war** (right `declare_war`, the king alone by default): the goal is tribute (vassalage, or `tributePermille` permille of income for `tributeDays` days), an indemnity (`indemnityPermille` permille of the treasury), a raid, land, a city or the throne (taken in the world by the next stage; the terms of peace record the claims). The war starts after `preparationMinutes`; both sides are told, and it shows in the tab, on the atlas and in the chronicle. The allies of the defender are called and answer for themselves (a refusal costs the relation); vassals and the overlord join at once. Not allowed: during a truce, during a pact or an alliance without breaking it (breaking it for war gives the enemy +15 of score), on one's own vassal or overlord (except a war for independence), on a kingdom of players with nobody online.
- **Score and exhaustion** of each side go from 0 to 100. Score comes from battles of squads won (8) and soldiers put down (1), enemies online killed in PvP (5), caravans robbed (6) and deals of the enemy blocked (3); holding the goal comes with the next stage. Exhaustion grows by `exhaustionPerDay` a day, faster with an empty treasury, and with losses and robbed caravans. A side worn out to 100 makes peace on the terms of the enemy by itself (paying what it has). After `maxWarDays` days a war ends in a white peace.
- **Peace.** The leading kingdoms offer terms by their goals: with a lead in score of `imposeLead` a side may ask its goal in full, with a smaller lead half of it (vassalage becomes a tribute), without a lead only a white peace; giving in is always possible. Terms apply together: the indemnity moves from the treasury of the loser into the reserves of the winner by the books (ledger line "War"), then the truce, vassalage, tribute and the claims of land, a city and the throne. If the loser cannot pay, there is no peace.
- **War far from players.** Squads ordered to hold, guard or patrol a place on the land of the enemy advance: every `stepSeconds`, with the chance `battleChance`, they meet a squad of the enemy that holds its land or stays home and fight by the battles of the army (losses, wounds, experience and wear as in the army). Without defenders a squad ravages the land. A squad in the field falls upon a caravan of the enemy with the chance `raidChance` on the mechanics of the ambushes of robbers: the guards fight, and if they lose a share of the cargo goes to the storehouses of the raiders (what does not fit is left on the road) and the payment for it back to the buyer. Deals between enemies are frozen, and a caravan of an enemy does not pass the land of a kingdom at war with it (a blocked delivery is no failure of the deal).
- **Near players.** Soldiers and guards of kingdoms at war fight enemy soldiers and enemy players online at the keyboard. Players of kingdoms at war may fight each other; players of kingdoms at peace may not (`protectPeacefulPlayers`; it does not protect the wanted). Citizens of the enemy may be hurt. Neither the law of the enemy nor one's own law counts blows and killings of an enemy as crimes; theft and the land stay protected as in peace (sieges and captures come with the next stage). Entering the land of an enemy shows a line above the hotbar.
- **Protection of the absent.** Players who are away or offline are hurt neither by soldiers nor by players. A kingdom of players with nobody online at the keyboard is shielded `absenceMinutes` minutes after the last one left (and not sooner than `fightGraceSeconds` after a fight near its players): no battles of squads on its land, its caravans are not robbed, no score grows against it and it does not tire. The atlas and the tab show it. Kingdoms of the world have no shield, their army defends them.
- **Rulers of the world.** A merchant looks for pacts and alliances, a builder for pacts, a warrior declares war on a weak neighbour (`npcStrength` times weaker), not more often than every `npcWarDays` days and not on a kingdom just out of a war: for land when it is a bordering state of the world twice weaker (see "Large states"), else for tribute or loot; a just ruler never attacks first but stands by its allies; a mage keeps out. They answer offers by character and relation (with the reason of a refusal), accept peace when tired or beaten, and offer peace or press their terms when far ahead. Wars on players come at most once every `npcPlayerWarDays` days per kingdom and only with `npcWarsOnPlayers`. Wars of kingdoms of the world go on abstractly and show in the chronicle, the rumors and on the atlas.
- **The "Diplomacy" tab** of the throne: kingdoms with relation and bond (actions: pact, alliance, vassalage, freedom, breaking, the goal and the declaration of war), offers and calls with their answers, wars (goal, time, score, exhaustion, allies, the terms that may be asked or given, buttons of peace), the chronicle. The atlas: a bar in the colour of the relation under the crown of a capital, a sign of war, alliance, pact, vassalage and truce, a blue frame around a shielded kingdom; the places of battles go to the "battles" layer of map mods. Taverns tell of wars, battles, raids and peace.
- **Load.** Diplomacy is counted once a second, a step of the wars every `stepSeconds`, the decisions of rulers once a game day; there are no new scans of the world (soldiers look for enemies only while their kingdom is at war).

### War in the world (sieges, destruction, capture)

- **Open land.** The land of a kingdom is open to the players of the other side only while the war goes on (not in preparation, not in a truce) and the kingdom is not shielded for its absent players (a kingdom of players has somebody online at the keyboard; kingdoms of the world have no shield, their army keeps them). Land of third kingdoms and unowned land are never touched. When the defenders leave, the shield comes by the rules of 9a and the siege stands still.
- **Destruction and loot.** On open land the enemy breaks and places blocks and opens chests and storehouses (loot of war, a little score for every chest); the law does not judge it. The throne and blocks of the tag `kingdomworld:unclearable` never break. Explosions break blocks on open land (`siege.explosions`), never on land of third kingdoms or under the shield. Blocks of registered buildings and walls broken in war are written down: after the war (or sooner, when no enemy came for `siege.repairQuietMinutes` minutes) builders put them back, paid with items from the storehouses. A ruler of the world orders what is missing from its workshops; a player sees in the tab what is missing. Buildings outside the register are not mended: the hint of the declaration of war and the tab warn of it.
- **Gates.** Gates of walls get leaves: fence gates (planks) on a palisade, an iron portcullis on a stone wall, four rows in the opening. Builders hang them from the storehouses (12 fence gates or 48 planks, 12 iron bars or 5 ingots) at the first check, on walls of old worlds too. In war, with an enemy within 24 blocks, gates close by themselves; they open for subjects and allies (within 4 blocks, while no enemy is close); an enemy cannot open them, only break them. Strength: planks `siege.woodGateStrength` (720), iron `siege.ironGateStrength` (1800). A soldier with the order "siege" at a gate takes 1 a second (six soldiers at most), a ram 3 (two at most): a squad with a ram breaks a palisade gate in 80 seconds and a portcullis in 3-4 minutes. A player with a ram in hand breaks leaves and walls faster. Broken leaves fall as items, the gate stands open until new ones are hung.
- **Ram** (recipe: two logs and a block of iron in a row). In the hands of a player it breaks leaves, logs and stone of walls faster; a siege brings rams (an operator command, or a ruler of the world builds them from two logs and nine iron ingots).
- **Orders of the army.** "Siege" (the marshal's baton in siege mode on a city of the enemy within 160 blocks): the squad goes to the nearest closed gate, strikes it, then to the square. "Defend" (the baton on an own city): the squad stands at the city. The marshal's right, like other orders.
- **Holds.** A claimed chunk of the enemy is occupied when attackers (players online or soldiers) are on it for `siege.occupyMinutes` minutes (3) without defenders; defenders free it by holding it as long. The land of provinces is held by provinces: one where people live at the square of its main settlement (`siege.plazaMinutes`), an empty one at its boundary stone (`siege.occupyMinutes`), see "Large states". A city is taken when attackers hold the square at the town hall (12 blocks) for `siege.plazaMinutes` minutes (5) without defenders. The throne is taken when the throne hall (8 blocks from the throne) is held for `siege.throneMinutes` minutes and the player king of the defenders was online in this war. Defenders coming interrupt the hold (the time starts over); under the shield of the absent it stands still. Taking gives score. Far from players squads count: a besieging squad strikes the gate by half its strength and after the breach holds the square while no squad of the defenders defends, holds or guards there.
- **Peace.** The goal "land": the chunks occupied by the winner go to it (no more than the terms, never the capital chunk), the others come back; from a kingdom with provinces the peace also hands over the provinces taken, whole (2 by full terms, 1 by halved ones, never the heart). "A city": the taken city with its land around (64 blocks), buildings, citizens (soldiers excepted, their mood falls) and wall goes to the winner; from a state of the world it is one province where people live with its settlement. "The throne": an annexation: the treasury (at its worth in the currency of the winner, reserves as they are), land, cities (the capital becomes a city of the winner), buildings and citizens go to the winner; players of the loser choose in the chat "Become a subject" or "Stay free" (`/kingdom conquest swear|free`, three days), the former king loses the crown and the chronicle tells it. With nothing taken the peace hands nothing over in the world. Everything goes by the steps of a journal: a restart in the middle neither loses nor doubles anything.
- **Bodies.** Near players soldiers of both sides fight as bodies (the goals of the army of 9a); one siege has at most `siege.maxBodies` bodies (60) per kingdom, the rest fight far from players.
- **Rulers of the world.** A warrior at war for a city or the throne besieges the nearest city of the enemy (or the capital) when its land is open: it builds a ram and sends half its squads; one siege at a time. A ruler whose city is besieged sends squads to defend it. The switch `npcWarsOnPlayers` of 9a holds.
- **Visibility.** The line above the hotbar: "Siege: gate 40%", "Holding the square: 3 of 5 minutes". The "Diplomacy" tab, mode "In the world": sieges, holds, occupied chunks, what peaces hand over, what is broken and what mending takes, what is missing, the warning about buildings outside the register. The atlas: occupied chunks hatched, a besieged city in a ring with the share of its gate broken, a taken city with a banner, details on hover. Chronicle and rumors: sieges, broken gates, captures, annexations, lost crowns.
- **Commands.** `/kingdomworld admin siege status|unlock|timing`, `start <attacker> <defender> [city] [rams]`, `stop`, `gate <kingdom> hang|break|list`, `hold <attacker> <defender> city|throne [city] [percent]`, `occupy <attacker> <defender>` (the chunk of the source, on the land of a province the whole province), `annex <winner> <loser>`, `cede <winner> <loser> <city>`, `repair <kingdom> [blocks]`, `count <kingdom>`.
- **Data** in `kingdomworld_siege.dat` (format 3): gates, sieges, holds, the journal of conquests, damage (of war and of property damaged in peace: format 2 added to the record of a block whether it comes back for free and the block an outsider placed over it; format 3 added holds and conquests of provinces with the step of settlements; formats 1 and 2 read as they are), which kings were online, the choices of players of annexed kingdoms. Data that cannot be read keeps sieges read-only.
- **Load.** Sieges are counted once a second; the game test measure: 30 against 30 soldiers as bodies next to one another, an average tick of 6 ms, the worst 23 ms.

### Nobility, factions, intrigue and espionage

- **Houses.** A noble house: a family (its name in both alphabets, quoted in Russian lines), a head with a character and a trait, a party, wealth and loyalty to the crown (0-100). A kingdom of the world has 2-5 houses from the first day of its court, with titles on its cities (counts) and marches (barons); the first house of a large kingdom is a duke. A kingdom of players has no houses until its king grants a title. Houses have no bodies; heads grow old and die, and the heir takes over.
- **Titles.** Baron, count, duke; a title always comes with a fief: a city of the kingdom or one of its four marches (the land of the kingdom north, east, south or west of the capital outside its cities). The holder gets a share of the income of the fief every day (`nobility.shareBaron` 10%, `shareCount` 15%, `shareDuke` 20% of yesterday's income, in the part of the citizens who live there): a player onto the account, a house into its wealth; all by the books (`Nobility` in the treasury journal). The king and the chancellor (right `manage_nobility`) grant a title to a house, a new house or a subject, take it back and hand it over in the Nobility tab. The law does not judge a noble player for theft, the large cities of the kingdom let them in free, and they get no rights over the treasury (like the honorary councillors of 8b). A ruler of the world makes a player a baron when they dealt well with it and have a high reputation in its land, at most once in 7 days.
- **Stability** 0-100: a base of 50, the loyalty of the houses, the mood of the citizens, the tax against the usual one, the weariness of war, defeats and lost cities of the last 10 days, a recent festival, the content of the parties and what happened lately (a refused demand, incitement, an execution, mercy, rebels, a city that left). The tab shows the causes. Low stability (below `nobility.plotBelow`, 30) spoils the mood of the citizens (they work and grow worse), high stability (from 70) cheers them.
- **Parties:** the war party, the merchants, the old nobility, the crown party. Houses join by their character; the influence of a party grows with its houses, their titles and wealth. At most once in `nobility.demandEveryDays` (3) played days the least content party demands: a lower tax, a war or a peace, a title, works, a share of the loot. The king answers with a button: agree (it is done and costs money), refuse (content and stability fall), bargain (the house gets half the price, half the offence). A demand not answered in `demandDueDays` (2) played days counts as a mild refusal. Rulers of the world answer by their traits and never start a war or make peace because a party asks.
- **A played day** of a kingdom of players is a game day on which one of its players was online. Demands, plots and rebels move only on such days: a player who comes back finds no catastrophe.
- **Plots.** When the stability stays below 30 for two played days, a discontented house (loyalty below 40) may start a plot; it ripens in `nobility.plotDays` (4) played days. Counter-intelligence (15% base, plus spies hunting foreign agents and an appointed spymaster) may find it out every day; then the king decides the fate of the house (execute the head, pardon, exile). A plot against a ruler of the world makes the head of the house the new ruler with their character (chronicle, rumors, the kingdom stays the same). Against a king of players a plot never takes the crown: rebels keep `rebelPercent` (25%) of the income for `rebelDays` (4) played days; only after long unrest (`secessionDays`, 6 played days of low stability) and a warning (after `secessionWarnDays`, 3) the city of the house leaves the kingdom: to the neighbour who backed the plot by incitement, or as a small kingdom of the world with the head of the house on its throne. The city goes through the journal of conquests of 9b (land, buildings, citizens, walls) without loss or duplication; it can be won back by war or diplomacy.
- **Spies.** The spymaster (right `manage_spies`, the king holds it too) hires spies among the agents of the guild (`nobility.spyHire` 100, a wage of `spyWage` 5 a day, at most `maxSpies` 4). Missions in another kingdom: scouting (treasury, soldiers, wars, the plans of the ruler, whether a plot brews), incitement (less stability; a plot there ripens faster and we back it), sabotage (food in the storehouses spoils), theft (5% of the treasury, at most 400, from their treasury into ours by the books), the murder of the head of a house (kingdoms of the world only). Success depends on the skill of the spy, the counter-intelligence of the target and the relations; a failure brings the spy back empty-handed or gets them caught (a ransom of `ransom` 150 within `ransomDays` 3 days, else death; proud and bold rulers of the world execute at once). A caught spy and a backed plot spoil the relations (reasons "their spy caught", "they backed a plot"), a reason for war. Missions against a kingdom of players with nobody online wait at its border. Rulers of the world keep 0-2 spies by their character and send them against enemies and neighbours they dislike.
- **Marriages of houses** of two kingdoms on good terms (chance `marriageChance` a day) raise the loyalty of the houses and the relations of the kingdoms (reason "a marriage of houses").
- **Visibility.** The throne tab "Nobility": houses (head, party, loyalty; titles and wealth on hover) with the choice of title and fief to grant; titles with fiefs and holders; the court (stability with its causes, parties, the demand with its buttons, plots found out with verdicts); the chronicle; for the secret service the spies (skill, mission, captivity), the choice of task and target, and reports. Messages of demands, plots found out, the warning of a city that may leave, rebels, the share of a fief. Rumors of taverns: marriages and deaths of the great, caught spies, hints of plots.
- **Commands.** `/kingdomworld admin nobility status|unlock|tick|day|timing`, `house <kingdom> [add]`, `house loyalty <house> <0-100>`, `title house|player <kingdom> <house|player> <baron|count|duke> <fief>` (fief `c<city id>` or `m<0-3>`), `title revoke <title>`, `stability <kingdom> [low <days>] [<cause> <amount> <days>]`, `faction <kingdom> <party> <content>`, `demand <house> <kind> [value]`, `demand answer <kingdom> accept|refuse|bargain`, `plot <house> <progress> [backer]`, `plot discover|verdict <kingdom> ...`, `spy list|hire|send|ransom ...`.
- **Data** in `kingdomworld_nobility.dat` (format 1): houses, titles, courts (content of parties, played days, days of unrest, recent events, chronicle, reports), demands, plots, spies. Data that cannot be understood keeps the nobility read-only. Old worlds get the rights `manage_nobility` (chancellor) and `manage_spies` (spymaster) with the upgrade `nobility_rights`.
- **Load.** The nobility moves once a game day: one kingdom a tick, without scanning the world, then the spies and the marriages.

### Installing on a server (exaroton)

- Put `kingdomworld-1.4.1.jar` into the `mods` folder of the server and into `mods` of every player: the server does not let in a client without the mod (or with another version), Forge lists the mods that do not match.
- Memory: 8-10 GB for a server with the owner's pack (61 mods); the mod itself takes some tens of megabytes in the measurements (see "Performance"). A server without other mods does with 3-4 GB.
- Settings of the world: `world/serverconfig/kingdomworld-server.toml` (made on the first start; change it while the server is stopped, or with `/reload` for some values). Common: `config/kingdomworld-common.toml`, a player's: `config/kingdomworld-client.toml`.
- What is usually changed:
  - `[war] npcWarsOnPlayers = false`: rulers of the world declare no wars on kingdoms of players (by default they do, rarely);
  - `[war] protectPeacefulPlayers`: players of kingdoms at peace cannot hurt each other;
  - `[siege] explosions = false`: explosions break no blocks even on open land of an enemy;
  - `[npcKingdoms]`: the plan (`plan`: `states` or `legacy`, acting only on a new world), how many kingdoms of the world the plan has, of which sizes (`sizeQuota`) and how far apart, how they appear as players come near;
  - `[nobility] maxHouses`, `demandEveryDays`, `secessionDays`: how much nobility there is, how often it demands, after how many days of unrest a city may leave;
  - `[economy] wages`: the wages of professions (new defaults go only into a new config file).
- Backups: the data of the mod lies in `world/data/kingdomworld_*.dat`; copy it with the world.
- Measuring the load of a live server: `/kingdomworld admin perf reset`, after half an hour of play `/kingdomworld admin perf report`.

### Performance

Measured with the smoke check and `-Load` (9-10 kingdoms of the world with houses and spies, 2 wars, a siege, caravans; 30 minutes with a turn of every ruler, a day of the wars and a day of the courts each minute) and `/kingdomworld admin perf`. "Tick handlers" are all handlers of the server tick: on a clean server the mod alone, in the pack other mods too.

| Measure | pack, before | pack, after | clean, after | clean, before the indexes | clean, after the indexes | clean, after the step in slices |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| server tick, average | 1.88 ms | 2.50 ms | 2.03 ms | 8.87 ms | 5.91 ms | 5.36 ms |
| server tick, worst | 430 ms | 254 ms | 298 ms | 1616 ms | 30 890 ms | 1590 ms |
| server ticks over 50 ms | 0.08% | 0.04% | 0.23% | 2.68% | 1.40% | 1.06% |
| tick handlers, average | 0.48 ms | 0.46 ms | 0.59 ms | 5.37 ms | 3.75 ms | 2.37 ms |
| tick handlers, worst | 424 ms | 248 ms | 296 ms | 1611 ms | 30 888 ms | 1588 ms |
| handler ticks over 50 ms | 17 | 8 | 53 | 571 | 327 | 265 |
| memory after GC | 552 of 6144 MB | 535 of 6144 MB | 275 of 2048 MB | 313 of 2048 MB | 299 of 2048 MB | 331 of 2048 MB |
| data of the mod / world | 51 KB / 81 MB | 53 KB / 86 MB | 58 KB / 87 MB | 260 KB / 136 MB | 289 KB / 134 MB | 288 KB / 145 MB |
| server start | 31 s | 31 s | 14 s | 19 s | 17 s | 16 s |

- The systems' own timings in the same run: the worst turn of rulers 2.4 ms, a step of wars 6.7 ms, a day of courts 1.7 ms.
- Indexes of storehouses, buildings and cities (stage 15): the columns "clean, before the indexes" and "clean, after the indexes" are the smoke `-Load` on the code before and after them, with the present set (8 kingdoms of the world with armies and camps). The worst tick of both is a step of a camp of an army that levels the ground and loads chunks (1.2 s before, 31 s after in another world), not the indexes. A step of the citizens of a kingdom of 976 citizens (a large kingdom of the world and 700 newcomers without a bed, the game test stand `IndexBenchGameTests`): 33.0 ms on average and 39.2 ms at worst before, 24.3 ms and 30.8 ms after.
- The step of citizens in slices and writing off the server thread (stage 15): the column "clean, after the step in slices" is the same smoke `-Load`. Its worst ticks come from building steps of rulers that level the ground (up to 0.3 s) and the loading of the world as the load begins, not from the step of citizens. The stand `ScaleGameTests` (3 kingdoms of 1700 citizens, 400 buildings and 40 storehouses): the beginning of the steps of all three 1.3 ms, then 40 slices of 0.40 ms on average and 0.85 ms at worst; the choice of bodies 0.04 ms; the tag of the file of the kingdoms (22 MB uncompressed) 20 ms on the server thread, while compressing and writing, which used to happen there too and took 98 ms with the tag, now run on their own thread.
- After the cache of workplaces (the last change) 10 minutes of the same load on a clean server: tick handlers 0.34 ms on average, worst 31 ms, none over 50 ms.
- The plan of states (stage 15) of a new world of radius 7000 is ready in less than a minute after the start: 40 s on a clean server (the survey 16 s, the thrones 7 s, the borders 8 s, the founding 6 s), 37 s with the pack; the worst tick of the plan is about 8.5 ms, and the server spends about 0.1 s on the plan in all. Before, with the noise worked out in the ticks of the server, the plan took 11 to 13 minutes.
- An alarm in a large capital of the world (the client showcase `-PclientSelfTest=npc`, an integrated server): the player on the square wounds 12 guards, the alarm goes up to the captain of the army, and after 40 seconds 59 of 62 soldiers stand by the player (20 of them of the army), 61 soldiers with a body: a server tick of 15.8 ms on average, the worst 48.3 ms, none over 50 ms of 900. Without an alarm inside the same capital, 137 bodies of citizens: 15.5 ms, the worst 38.3 ms.
- A large army (the client showcase, an integrated server; the background appearance of other kingdoms of the world is held while a scene is measured, its slow ticks while chunks load in the same showcase without the hold: 102 ticks over 50 ms, 73 ms on average, the worst 496 ms). Inside a large capital, 162 bodies: 13.3 ms, the worst 35.1 ms. An alarm up to the captain of the army, 84 soldiers by the player: 16.6 ms, the worst 75.8 ms, 2 ticks over 50 ms of 900. The camp of a large kingdom in peace, 15 bodies of soldiers: 10.5 ms, the worst 34.1 ms, none over 50 ms. A battle of the armies of a large and a small kingdom near the player, 52 bodies of soldiers: 13.0 ms, the worst 37.0 ms, none over 50 ms of 1000 (before a soldier walked a long way a stretch at a time, a way to a far point of a march loaded chunks from the disk on the spot: 20-31 ms, the worst 543 ms, 63 ticks over 50 ms).
- The background appearance of the kingdoms of the world (the client self-check `-PclientSelfTest=background -PquickPlayWorld=<a new world>`: the player stands still while all kingdoms of the plan appear; both runs recorded with JFR). A tick is measured whole: the game times a tick before the handlers of its end, where the mod works, so `/kingdomworld admin perf`, the client check and the appearance itself count from the first handler of a tick to its last one, and the worst tick of an appearance leaves out the work of other systems of the mod and the pauses of the garbage collector. Before: all 8 kingdoms in 634 s, 379 ticks of the game and the appearance over 50 ms (without the autosave), the worst 391 ms. After: 865 s, 14 ticks, the worst 58 ms; the worst tick of the appearance of each place 16-35 ms. What it was: the plan worked out the noise of the generator in the tick; a chunk counted as loaded as soon as its ticket applied, and the first block read from it waited in the tick for it to load or generate; a search of a road loaded chunks on the spot, and its map of cells put neighbouring cells into the same buckets; a large building was laid out in one tick (a castle 130 ms); the army was recruited and settled all at once. Now the plan asks two threads of its own for the heights, chunks are waited for, a building is laid out in parts of 8 ms and the army is recruited a part at a time.
- The camp of an army and the buildings of rulers under the load of the smoke check `-Load` (30 minutes, a clean server): before, the worst server tick 1511 ms, 2.16% of ticks over 50 ms, 20 slow ticks of the rulers (a step of a camp up to 294 ms, laying out a house up to 935 ms); after, 149 ms, 0.44%, no tick of the rulers over 50 ms with 9 camps raised.
- The game test of a siege of thirty against thirty (60 bodies): 12.4 ms, the worst 24.9 ms. It used to waver near its limit of 50 ms: 77% of the time went to items that fell out of storehouses when other tests cleared their platforms; the clearing removes them now.
- Made lighter: a soldier looks through the storehouses once for all its gear instead of once a slot; the price table is built again inside `/reload`, not in the first tick after it; a ruler works out at most one new cost of an upgrade a look; the search for workplaces in chunks remembers the answer for each block state (the busiest place of the mod in a JFR recording).
- Styled capitals (stage 14d), the smoke check on a dedicated server, two clean runs and two with `-Pack`:
  - the plan of the world finds its 8 places on its third or fourth pass, 89-136 s after the start (44-67 s before: a styled capital needs a wider square of level land; the smoke check gives the plan 240 s);
  - a large styled capital appearing from the start: 90-124 buildings with its towns, 271-284 citizens, 169-207 soldiers, 1168-2959 ticks, 3.8-6.8 ms on average, the worst 18-26 ms;
  - a small kingdom raised in the background: 3.3-5.5 ms on average, the worst 9.6-24.6 ms, no tick waited;
  - the rest of a large appearance after a restart: 8.1-9.6 ms on average, the worst 50-150 ms at the steps of the ground and the buildings (their budget counts blocks, not time; an open item);
  - the turns of 15 rulers: the worst 8.5-15.6 ms; diplomacy the worst 2.3-22.6 ms, the steps of the wars 3.4-26.9 ms, a day of the courts 2.2-3.2 ms.
  - Raising a kingdom of the world counts a chunk as come in only once the whole of it is ready, and so do the jobs of rulers: before, the check of the land and towns of a raise and the levelling of a ruler's house waited on the server thread while the chunk was generated (stops of seconds in a raise, ruler ticks of 236-436 ms; now the rulers at most 15.6 ms). A large capital takes longer to load its land for it: 766 chunks in about 28 s instead of 461 chunks in 6 s.
- Styled capitals in the client showcase (`-PclientSelfTest=npc`, an integrated server with the client drawing, a fresh copy of the smoke world):
  - the large northern capital (guild SS) appearing by the player: 108 buildings, 226 citizens, 150 soldiers, 3 towns, 1711 ticks, 12.75 ms on average, the worst 296.7 ms when its castle is laid (a template is laid in one tick: an open item);
  - inside the large capital with 62-123 bodies: 11.2-15.1 ms on average, the worst 21-46 ms;
  - the small terem capital raised in the background: 43 buildings, 139 citizens, 101 soldiers, 14.3 ms on average, the worst 57 ms; the other kingdoms of the plan in the background 12.7-16.2 ms on average, the worst 29.7-104.9 ms (the castle of a small kingdom laid);
  - an alarm with 70 soldiers within 24 blocks: 18.4-18.9 ms on average, the worst 70.9-105.7 ms, 1-4 ticks over 50 ms of 900; a camp in peace 11.5 ms, the worst 19.5 ms; a battle of 56 soldiers 14.1 ms, the worst 61.8 ms, 5 ticks over 50 ms of 1000.
- Styled capitals in the game tests: a large northern capital rises in 2.9 s of work (71 buildings); in 30 days of life it keeps its 266 citizens, nobody starves.
- Tactics of squads (the client check `-PclientSelfTest=tactics`, integrated server, night in a large capital of the world): 50 monsters against the garrison near the player (57-58 bodies of soldiers) give an average tick of 24.4 ms without the tactics, the worst 109.8 ms, 45 ticks over 50 ms of 700; the same fight with the tactics 22.3 ms, 25 ticks over 50 ms (the worst, 362 ms, fell with a turn of the rulers, 102.9 ms by their log, and a camp built at once), decisions of leaders 0.21 ms each, the worst tick of the tactics 19.5 ms, one tick over 5 ms. An alarm on the square with 56 soldiers with bodies: 13.8 ms, the worst 56.6 ms, the tactics no more than 0.42 ms a tick.

### Balance

A model of a treasury over 60 days (`KingdomBudget`, a unit test): the poll tax of subjects, what merchants sell abroad, wages, mercenaries, works paid from the treasury, shares of the nobles, spies, a tribute.

| Kingdom | income a day | expense a day | after 60 days |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| small, of players | 125 | 41 | 5,240 |
| large, of players | 386 | 308 | 6,680 |
| merchant of the world | 461 | 164 | 19,320 |
| warrior of the world at war with mercenaries | 346 | 514 | below zero; a week of war fits its savings |
| builder of the world | 230 | 182 | 4,380 |

- Treasuries at peace grow slowly and do not go bankrupt; a band of a lieutenant (271 a day) is affordable for a week of war and ruins a kingdom if kept longer; a tribute of 20% of the income and the ransom of a spy (150) are a few days of income.
- Defaults: the wage of merchants 0 became 8, innkeepers 0 became 6, registrars 0 became 7, mages 0 became 12 (they worked for nothing, though merchants bring most of the income of a treasury). Soldiers are paid 15 by the settings of the army already. New values go into new config files; add them to `[economy] wages` of an existing one.

### Compatibility with the pack

- The owner's pack (61 mods) passes the smoke check `-Pack` without errors of the mod, with 30 minutes of load.
- **Create and other machines acting as fake players:** the protection of land lets them by `allowFakePlayers`; the law does not judge a fake player (it is no player), soldiers and guards leave it alone.
- **Better Combat:** blows of players and soldiers go through the usual damage of Minecraft, so the law, war and the protection of the absent work the same; soldiers choose weapons of mods by their damage and speed.
- **Jack's Gravestones:** at an execution the guards take the items before the mod of graves (see "Law and guards").
- **Waystones and other teleports:** teleporting onto the land of another kingdom is no crime in itself. Teleporting into a large walled city past its gates is trespassing only for an outsider who owes the entry fee and has no pass (or is wanted); subjects, nobles, holders of a pass and small cities without a fee are not touched.
- **A client without the mod** does not get into a server with the mod: Forge compares mods on joining and lists what does not match (`displayTest = MATCH_VERSION`).

### Requirements

- Minecraft 1.20.1
- Forge 47.4.10 or newer in the 47.x line
- Java 17
- Install the mod on both the server and the client. Versions must match, otherwise the connection is refused.
- No other mods are required. Integrations with other mods (Create, map mods and so on) switch on automatically, only when that mod is installed.

The mod is built for dedicated servers: all logic and every state change run on the server only; the client only displays data and sends intents that the server validates (rights, distance to the throne, request size and rate limits).

### Building

```
gradlew build
```

The jar ends up in `build/libs/kingdomworld-<version>.jar`. JDK 17 is required; if the system `java` is a different version, set `JAVA_HOME` first (see the Russian section for an example). The first build downloads and prepares Minecraft, which takes a few minutes.

### Tests

| What | Command |
|---|---|
| Core unit tests and architecture checks | `gradlew test` (part of `gradlew build`) |
| Forge GameTests | `gradlew runGameTestServer` |
| Smoke test on a clean dedicated server | `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/smoke-server.ps1` |
| Smoke test with the modpack's mods | `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/smoke-server.ps1 -Pack` |

Core unit tests cover claiming and giving up chunks (connectivity, limits, distance to other kingdoms), rights of positions, membership transitions and the access policy, and citizens: names in both alphabets, beds and workplaces, death and the chronicle, growth, food, mood, the profession registry and the choice of citizens with a body. The translation test checks that `en_us.json` and `ru_ru.json` hold the same keys with non-empty values and the same number of arguments, that Russian values are written in Russian, and that every key the code uses exists. All text a player sees goes through translation keys. Production unit tests check the choice of recipes, tag ingredients, sub-orders, loops and the depth limit of chains, reservations, keep quotas and the formulas of the workshops. Money unit tests check item values (recipes, tags as choices, cycles, the default), reading amounts, coins and change, the journal of the treasury, payments from the treasury with change, wages when money is short, taxes and growing debts, the Treasury right, old currencies, overflows and the plural forms of Russian and English. The architecture tests check that the `core` package does not depend on Minecraft, Forge or Mojang, that client code is only reached through guarded entry points and that compat modules are not referenced directly. Trade unit tests check the price curve and the spread, that exporting is always cheaper than importing, the tax on sales, rates and their smoothing, that exchanges neither make nor lose money (a property test over random exchanges), escrow of deals and lost caravans, imports within the daily limit, lots, the rights of the market and of deals, paying with coins and change, and the plural forms of items, days and minutes. Guild unit tests check the ranks of monsters from their attributes, bosses and overrides, the chance of crystals and the guard against farms and spawners, joining and points, exams and the wait after a failed one, the life of a contract and its escrow (payment less the commission, the escrow back when a contract runs out or is called off, lost points, the posting fee, the daily budget of the guild), handing in items, squads and how expeditions end with wounds and deaths through `TaskRisks`; the translation test checks the plural forms of monsters, bosses, points and crystals. Construction unit tests check turning and mirroring of positions and directions (together, the way the game does), the order of work (clearing from the top down, then full and leaning blocks from the bottom up), the cost of templates (doors, beds, double slabs, candles, liquids, blocks without an item), the state machine of constructions with their rights and limits, work far from players (a block paid whole, time carried over, experience) and the plural forms of blocks; another test checks that the built-in templates in the resources match what the generator draws, byte for byte. Unit tests of buildings check the open register of roles (the mod's roles, roles of code and of data packs, code winning), the numbers of roles by level and a template's own numbers, the threshold of integrity and the states of a role (works, damaged, demolished, off our land, none), the role coming back after a repair, comfort and growth with beds in houses, the difference of templates for an upgrade (the same untouched, the rest taken out, only the new paid), the plan and cost of a repair, what a demolition gives back with the loss (block by block as much as in one go) and its order, the table of woods (the game's woods and those of an invented mod, nether stems and hyphae, a block a species lacks, stones, the wood of a biome) and the check of an own blueprint (name, size, unbreakable blocks, empty, a bed for a house, a container for a warehouse, an unknown role, the limit).

`runGameTestServer` runs the game tests in a separate world (`run-gametest/`), a new one of the same seed every time (`-PgameTestSeed` sets another, with `-PkeepGameTestWorld` the tests run in the world of the last run), without mob spawning and weather, and exits with a non-zero code if any test fails. They include founding through the API, protection (an outsider cannot break the blocks of a kingdom none of whose players is online, opens a chest and a fence gate but pulls no lever, a member with the right can do all of it), an explosion in kingdom land, claiming and giving up chunks, and read-only mode for data of a newer version and for a damaged format version; a citizen's body comes from its record and leaves without duplicates, death changes the record and writes the chronicle, food is taken from a storehouse, a newcomer arrives with a bed and food, the farmer harvests, replants and stores, hoppers put into and take from a storehouse, citizens and storehouses survive saving, and format 2 data is upgraded without loss; a smelter fills a vanilla furnace with raw metal and fuel and takes out the ingot, a cook cooks food, a crafter makes what was ordered at the crafting table, a smith upgrades to netherite at the smithing table keeping the enchantments and makes an iron sword on order through the chain from raw iron, a reservation keeps two workers off one ingredient, a keep order holds its quota, special recipes are left out, making things far from players neither duplicates nor loses anything with full storehouses, orders and workshops survive saving and format 4 data is upgraded; a minter far from players and near them strikes coins from ingots of the storehouses and leaves metal a keep order holds, plenty of copper does not hold up striking silver from iron, the bank takes and pays out coins without duplicates or losses (also with a full inventory), players pay each other, wages are paid and an empty treasury spoils the mood, the tax is taken and debts grow, the server checks the Treasury right, item values come from tags and recipes, treasuries and accounts survive saving, format 5 data is upgraded, and damaged money makes the data read-only. Trade: a player buys at a stall with coins and from the account, the kingdom buys within its budget, a lot sells with the tax for the treasury and is taken back, merchants sell surplus abroad within the daily limit, goods from abroad arrive after the delay, an exchange keeps the money of both currencies and stays within the treasury, old coins melt into metal, a caravan delivers and pays and survives saving, a lost caravan gives the payment back, the server checks the Market and Deals rights, coins of the vault reach the king, and trade survives saving and the upgrade from format 6. The guild: a monster killed by a player leaves a crystal of its rank, animals and monsters killed without a player leave nothing; monsters from a spawner give less and less; a player joins at a desk kept by a registrar and gets rank F; a hunt counts kills and pays from escrow with the commission for the treasury; a contract that runs out gives the escrow back and costs points; items are handed in all at once or not at all; an exam raises the rank; a squad goes on an expedition without bodies, takes weapons from the storehouse and comes back with crystals; a death on an expedition goes to the chronicle; the server checks the right "Adventurers' guild"; the guild survives saving, data of a newer version stays read-only and the marshal gets the right once. A caravan that waits too long for room closes its deal with the payment and the goods going back. Construction: a builder near players builds a small template from the materials of a storehouse, clearing sends drops to the storehouse and stops at a container with items, bedrock, a spawner, reinforced deepslate and other protected blocks are never broken, whether laying out, far from players or by an operator, nothing is broken or placed on land lost before the place is loaded and what was paid for is built once the land is back, what is missing is shown and ordered from the workshops, cancelling gives the materials back without duplicates, a storehouse and a post of a template register as if a player placed them, the server refuses foreign land, a throne inside the building, overlaps and players without the right, a template with a block of a missing mod, with bedrock or too large cannot be built while the built-in ones can, work far from players pays for blocks, places them when their place is loaded and survives saving, format 7 data is upgraded, and stairs turn the way the core turns directions. Buildings: a house gives homes, comfort and room for newcomers; the containers of a warehouse become storehouses and stop being so when it is damaged; a damaged workshop loses its role, a builder far from players repairs it with materials from the storehouse and the role comes back; an upgrade changes only the difference, leaves a chest with things untouched and pays only for what is new; a demolition gives materials back with the loss and what a chest holds whole, and one cancelled half way leaves the building damaged; an own blueprint without a bed or with bedrock is not saved, a saved one survives saving and is built; a template is built of spruce with a cost in spruce and stairs keeping their facing; a subject without the right changes nothing, and a demolition on lost land breaks nothing; format 8 is upgraded and format 9 keeps buildings, constructions and blueprints. Roads: builders lay a section from the gravel of the storehouse (the turf goes to the storehouse), a road is not laid on foreign land without consent, a player is faster on a road, a caravan takes a road and arrives sooner, an ambush takes goods into the camp and gives the payment back, a player clears a camp and takes its chest (the blocks of the camp drop nothing), a robber breaks no blocks and spares a player away from the keyboard, the server checks rights, roads, camps, walls and remembered consents survive saving; builders put up a palisade of logs from the storehouse around a city on the lay of the land with gates where a road crosses it, the wall and its gates enter the register as fortifications and lose integrity when broken, and a player coming into the city through a gate and past the gates raises the event; the wall plays the role of a wall and work by template is refused for it; an escaped slave runs to the nearest camp, becomes a robber and brings its pack into the loot. Life of cities: a citizen goes to the tavern in the evening, drinks from the storehouse and is in a better mood; at night it sleeps in its own bed and gets up in the morning, freeing the bed; a sleeper does not see a theft, a blow close by wakes it and it sees the offender; the innkeeper sells bread from the storehouse, the money goes to the treasury and none is made from nothing, the tavern sells no diamond; a rumor of a camp points at the real camp (direction, distance, strength), its mark is bought once and appears on the atlas; an ill citizen goes to the infirmary and gets well; a festival takes its price from the treasury and its treat from the storehouse, enters the chronicle, raises the mood and is not repeated the same day. The core unit tests `LifeRulesTest` check the day with its spread, the choice of rumors, the chance of illness and getting well, festivals (mood, price, the day of the founding) and fairs (days and prices), and `TranslationKeysTest` the plural forms of the life of cities. The guard and the army (`GuardArmyGameTests`): the body of an officer has the health and the issued gear of its post and is not cut to 20 by the sync, without the armor of its post an officer is as strong as a fighter; the blow of the captain of the army is that of its sword and its post; the issued gear of a killed captain does not fall, that of a fighter falls without the mark of the issue; an alarm with guards out of the fight brings the vice-captain, the captain, the army with half of its fighters and the captain of the army; after the alarm a guard goes back to its post; the best fighter takes the post of a fallen officer after the days of the rules and only when the kit is paid; a king gives a post, which counts only in armor of the post; army data of format 2 gets its branches by the orders of the squads, and an unknown branch or post does not shut the army; issued gear does not reach the storehouses with its mark when a profession changes; a blow a shield took stays an assault; the shield is tried once for a blow. The core unit tests `ForceRulesTest` (a fight of one against one by the formulas of the game: who loses to the officers and who may win, the multipliers, the price of a kit), `ReliefTest` (the steps of the relief by their thresholds) and `ArmyStaffTest` (branches, posts, the fallen, the successor, the branches of old data, the wage of an officer, the full health of a citizen). The garrison of the kingdoms of the world: in a raised small capital ten of the guard and six of the army with four officers in kits issued for nothing sleep in the barracks and stand at their posts (two inside the storehouse, a round about it with the vice-captain, two at every gate, the captain in the hall of the throne, the army in reserve), a second stationing moves nobody, a gate that lost a guard is filled from a less important post, the posts survive saving; a kingdom of the first layout takes what stands in for named posts and fills its ranks by two recruits a day. The thirty days without players (`NpcLifeGameTests`) check the garrison as well: it is whole, its officers are there, the treasury does not melt.

Unit tests of the army check the strength of fighters (the strength of the guild's adventurers unchanged included), abstract battles (an empty side, even sides, an overwhelming side, the loser suffers more, the same random numbers give the same battle, battles without risk), guards lowering the chance of losing a caravan, the choice of gear by attributes with overrides and bans, recruiting and dismissing with the "Army" right, squads and orders, hiring and unpaid mercenaries leaving without making money, the limit of mercenaries, ranks and the cap of training, and the translation test the plural forms of soldiers, mercenaries, fighters, enemies and squads. Game tests of the army: a recruit takes the best weapon and armor of the storehouse and brings them back when dismissed, a soldier kills a monster on the land, a soldier never attacks a foreign player in peace, an unpaid band leaves and no money appears, guards save a caravan from an ambush, a fallen soldier goes to the chronicle, the server checks the "Army" right, the army survives saving, newer data stays read-only, the marshal gets the right once.

The smoke script installs a real Forge 1.20.1-47.4.10 server into `D:\Projects\kingdom-world-testenv` (outside the repository), adds the built jar, accepts the EULA and starts the server twice on a fresh world. The first launch plays the scenarios of every stage one after another and leaves prepared what must outlive a restart: a caravan on its way, paused constructions, a war, a siege with the square held 40%, a kingdom of the world in the middle of its appearance. The second launch, the only restart, compares all of it with what the first one left and finishes what was begun. A clean run takes about 5-7 minutes, with `-Pack` about 6-8; the parts of the launches and their times are printed on the way. The first launch founds the kingdom `SmokeTest` with a throne at 8 70 8 (`kingdomworld admin found`), claims chunk [2, 0], checks that a detached chunk is refused, adds three citizens, kills one by command and checks that two remain and none has a body without players. Then it sets up a smelter post at a furnace and a smithy at a crafting table, stores raw iron, coal and a stick, orders an iron sword and checks that after simulation steps the sword is in the storehouse and a keep order for bread stays; with `-Pack` it also finds a tool or weapon of a mod of the pack with a crafting table recipe, stores its ingredients and checks that the smith makes it on order. Then the treasury gets 1000 from an operator command, a minter far from players strikes 10 silver coins from 5 iron ingots of the storehouse, a payday pays the wages, the worth of every item (of the pack's mods too with `-Pack`) is worked out without errors and an iron sword is valued by its recipe. The second launch checks that the kingdom, its id, all 10 chunks, the same citizens, the posts, the sword, the order and the treasury's books, vault and funds survived, with nothing lost or doubled (exactly the same numbers). The script exits with code 1 if the server fails to start or stop, if the logs contain errors or exceptions related to `kingdomworld`, if fire protection is not active, if a client class of the mod is loaded on the server, or if the data does not survive the restart. Logs of each launch are kept in `smoke-logs` inside the server folder. Commands are written to the server console as UTF-8 without a BOM, so the script also works under code page 65001. Then a second kingdom, SmokePartner, is founded to the east (640 70 8) and accepts a deal for 32 wheat: the caravan leaves with the payment in escrow, a test buyer buys wheat at the market from the account, and the money of both currencies adds up. The second launch checks that the caravan survived the restart on its way, brings it in (`admin trade arrive`) and checks that the wheat is in the buyer's storehouse, the payment in the seller's reserves and that no money appeared. Construction: in the first launch the server loads the templates (the built-in ones and the one of the script's data pack that needs Create: with `-Pack` it is built, on the clean server it cannot be built and names the mod), a builder's post and a builder appear on a flattened place of chunk [2, 0], a small house is laid out, its materials go into the storehouse and the builder builds half of it far from players, and the construction is paused before the restart; in the second launch the construction is in its place with the same numbers and, let go on, the builder finishes the house, it enters the register and its materials in the storehouse add up with nothing left over.
