Buildings (chunk [-1, 1]): a well of birch built by an operator plays its role, loses blocks and rests, a builder far from players repairs it with materials from the storehouse and the role comes back, then a demolition removes it from the register and brings birch and stone to the storehouse; a hut that stands in the world is saved as an own blueprint, a construction from it waits paused over the restart with the same numbers, is finished in the second launch and enters the register as a house, with the materials adding up. With `-Pack` a small house is built of the first wood of a mod of the pack: its planks are in the cost and the building is whole by its own blocks. The life of cities in the smoke test (chance illnesses and fairs by themselves are off there): on flattened chunk [-1, -1] an operator builds a tavern, it plays its role, a new citizen becomes the innkeeper at the post behind the counter and the tavern is open; the innkeeper tells rumors; a citizen falls ill; a festival takes exactly its price from the treasury and its treat from the storehouse, and the money adds up; the fair of the capital opens. In the second launch the life of cities is loaded, the ill citizen is still ill, the tavern is open and a second festival the same day is refused without touching the money.

Unit tests of companions and parties check the split of rewards without losing a coin (on random shares too), the shared progress of a contract of a party, hand-ins in parts and contracts leaving with their holder, invitations, the limit and the lead, the offers of the market (the same for a day, new in a new period) and the price by strength, the limit by rank, buying (the money goes to the treasury, none appears), a closed market and the law, hunger, the warning and the escape, gifts and good keeping, freedom, wages and leaving unpaid, selling from the treasury, experience and levels. Game tests: buying a slave, a companion follows and kills a monster, never hurts a foreign player, the pack survives the owner leaving and writing and reading the data without a copy, a hungry slave warns and runs away, freedom into service and into a kingdom, a party of two splits a reward, friendly fire off, a king closes the market, packets.

Unit tests of the law check the rules of crime (king, official, owner, right, war and nobility as exemptions, subjects answer), thefts and blows adding up to one crime that costs reputation once, the fine of twice a theft with the multiplier of the court, execution and its replacement by a fine, the right of the court and fines that may only be lowered, the pardon, petty records fading with reputation (a murder never), the plan of a fine (coins, account, debt), debts paid back from income without making money, the ransom rounded up, the life of confiscated goods, reputation within its bounds and from trade, its weight on prices and the entry fee, the decision at a gate and the pass for a day, the laws of the court within their bounds; the translation test checks the plural forms of times, crimes, days and minutes. Game tests of the law: a theft from a storehouse seen by a citizen makes the thief wanted once reported, an unseen one does not; a witness killed before the report tells nothing; the king and an official take from the storehouse without crime, a subject without the right does not; a guard beats a thief down (alive, the fine taken from the coins, the money adds up) and a poor thief ends with a debt; an executed murderer loses the goods into confiscation and the coins into the treasury, the ransom of half their worth gives back exactly those goods (the named sword too) and cannot be paid twice; guards leave a player away from the keyboard alone and killing them is no head for a hunter; a pardon clears the record, a thief cannot pardon himself; the contract for a head is done by killing the wanted, the hunter is paid and is no murderer; the ban of slavery frees a foreign slave and fines the owner; the law survives saving, and goods of a mod that is gone keep it read-only. Unit tests of the law in a city: the rules of an alarm (raised at once with the offender and the place, lasting from the last cause, striking without calling out while blows fall and after a killing, what nobody reported handed over once to the soldier who sees the offender, the count of soldiers killed and wounded), the fine for damaged property and blocks broken one after another making one crime, the fine for a theft from the treasury and thefts from different victims kept apart, the victim a record names, records of damage in peace next to damage of war and how they come back, the share of a wage that goes home and how it becomes food and coins, the choice of the way to run. Game tests of the law in a city: an outsider opens a door, a trapdoor and a fence gate with anything in hand and leaves a house, and does not pull a lever; a blow frightens the citizen struck, the one who saw it and the one who heard it behind a wall, they run, a soldier and a citizen far away do not, and the fright ends; a killing nobody saw raises the alarm, a soldier from a post 40 blocks away runs up, writes the murder into the record itself and strikes without calling out; a soldier answers a blow nobody else saw; a player in creative mode is answered the same way, and left alone with `pursueCreative` off; a block of a city breaks without an item and comes back, with a witness it is one crime for both blocks, breaking it again gives nothing again, a block an outsider placed is taken out as an item, a chest and the throne stay whole, outside the city a block breaks freely, the land of a kingdom of players who are away and the `forbidden` mode do not break; an outsider places plain blocks only (a hopper under a chest, containers, redstone and rails are refused), and a minecart, an armor stand, a frame, a crystal, a boat and a spawn egg are placed neither by a click on a block nor by a click in the air; a torch, sand and a door on a broken block go with it without items and come back after it, a block with a frame or a sign on it does not break, with a full book of damage nothing breaks and the old records stay; outside the city a block with a frame on it does not break either, and without the frame it breaks freely; a share of a wage lies in the chest of the house as coins and bought bread without making money, the chest of a house is no storehouse, a theft from it is recorded as a theft from the citizen; a theft from a storehouse next to a soldier behind a wall makes the thief wanted at once, with the fine of the treasury and an alarm.

The law in the smoke test: a thief who is offline is wanted for a theft of 120 and pays the fine of 240 from an account of 100, the remaining 140 becomes a debt, the reputation drops; another player is wanted for a blow; a murderer (a stand-in with a sword, bread and a chestplate) is executed: 18 items in confiscation, nothing with the body, with `-Pack` Jack's Gravestones makes no grave; the money of the kingdom grew exactly by what the operator gave. After the restart the wanted, the confiscated goods, the debt, the reputation and the pass are the same, the money adds up, and the murderer buys exactly the 18 items back at the throne for half their worth.

The law of the cities in the smoke test: a small house is built on levelled ground inside the square of the capital of SmokeTest and a newcomer moves in. Strangers who are offline act through stand-ins, by the same events a real player raises (`admin law act`): one opens the door of the house and shuts it; another takes a loaf from the barrel of the house under the eyes of the resident, and the record names a theft from the house of that citizen; a third breaks a block of the wall: the block is gone without a drop, is written down as damaged property and on the record, waits while the stranger is in the house and comes back for free once the stranger has left; a fourth strikes the resident in the open: the alarm is raised, a warrior of the kingdom who stood away comes running to the offender, and the blow is on the record. Right before the server stops a stranger breaks one more block and stays in the house. After the restart the law data is read in format 2 with the same records (the theft still names the house of the citizen, the damaged property and the blow are there), the record of the block broken before the stop was read from the saved data, and the block comes back for free.

Companions in the smoke test: a player who is offline gets money on the account and buys a slave (the price goes to the treasury, no money appears), puts iron into the pack and forms a party; with `-Pack` the companion takes a weapon of a mod of the pack over an iron sword. After the restart the companion, its pack, its weapon and the party are the same.

Roads in the smoke test: in the first launch the city Smoke Hollow is founded at 40 100 8, the script levels the wild land between the kingdoms (a strip along z = 8 from x 46 to 591, and the land around the city), the operator plans a path along it (`admin roads road plan`, the way searched on the server over ticks), pays for it and places it in the world, a camp of robbers appears far from the way (5 robbers and a leader), `admin roads gear` arms robbers (with `-Pack` with weapons and armor of the pack's mods); the caravan of the deal takes the road (`admin roads caravans`: its way on roads). Caravans are slow in the smoke test (`caravanBlocksPerMinute = 20`), and no camp appears or grows by itself. In the second launch the city, the road with the same blocks paid, the camp and the way of the caravan are the same; the cities and roads of the whole world are compared as "no fewer than before the restart", because the kingdoms of the world have cities and roads of their own. The world is new every time: if no way is found along the strip (water ran into it, say), the road checks are skipped with a warning. A palisade goes up around Smoke Hollow (`wallRadius = 4` in the smoke test): the ring on the lay of the land with a gate where the road leaves, paid with logs from the storehouses, placed and entered into the register of buildings, and a gate cut by hand on the north side; after the restart the wall is the same. If the city stands in water in this world, the wall checks are skipped with a warning. After the check of the mint the mint goes, so that later steps of the simulation do not move the money between the checks.

Unit tests of the kingdoms of the world check that the plan of places is the same for the same seed, keeps capitals apart and away from the spawn, shifts and skips taken places and goes on from where it stopped; the choice of the size by weights and what a kingdom is made of (a small one stays a village, a medium one is a town, a large one a large city, everyone has a home, no more work than people, plots are taken once, fronts look at the throne); names without repeats in both alphabets; rulers with one or two traits that agree; laws by the character of the ruler; the wealth at the start (the treasury, food for the days asked, gear for every soldier); plural forms. Since stage 14 they check the quota (3 large and 5 small kingdoms for many seeds, the passes of the plan asking less and a smaller kingdom taking the place of a larger one), that a capital of districts of every size fits inside its wall for many seeds without buildings overlapping, that variants of templates and looks are the same for the same seed, beds and professions by the role of a building, the guard posts of the plan, and the catalog of templates against the files of the generator. Game tests build a platform at height 290 and check that a small kingdom appears (a throne, land, buildings with roles standing on the ground, citizens with homes and work, soldiers with an order, a treasury that agrees with its "Founding" journal, a palisade), that an appearance leaves the land and buildings of others alone and a place where players spent hours, that a town where players spent hours is left out and the blocks of the player stay, that a restart in the middle and a second appearance double nothing, that the market of a kingdom of the world sells to a player and that a player becomes its subject; since stage 14 also that a small capital of districts rises whole, the palace stands with the throne inside, a variant of a template works as its role, a restart in the middle goes on with the same composition, a large capital rises whole with its castle, the old grid layout still rises, and a small and a large kingdom live 30 game days without players (the steps of the citizens, the paydays and the turns of the ruler of every day): the population does not fall, nobody starves, the treasury does not run dry. Since stage 14d the unit tests check every styled plan (8 styles, 3 sizes, the ranks of the size, 10 seeds): the same plan for the same seed, the castle of the style and size around the throne, the wall radius, no buildings overlapping and all inside the wall, the guild house and the mage tower of the rank once on the square, the treasury, the court, the infirmary and both yards once, one wall gate per gate, at least 3 decorations, fronts looking at a way, trees and front gardens off the buildings, all guard posts inside the wall and the castle posts inside the castle, bunks for the guard and the army, a worker for every trade, all four quarters of houses in a large capital; a golden test keeps the capital of districts as it was; the choice of a style by climate without repeats, the rank of a guild by size, the camp of a style, what a ruler of a style may build, the colors of a kingdom as dyes, and the catalog of 247 templates against the files of the generator (every look block a block of the game). The game tests (the platform at height 230 now) raise a styled small capital whole with its castle, institutions, workers and bank, a large one of the northern style with its gates, royal barracks, castle posts, a worker in every trade and workers in both yards, go on with a styled capital after a restart, give 8 plantings 8 different styles, keep a style through saving, found a kingdom with a style and change it for exactly the fee (a member who is not king, the same style and an empty treasury refused); the 30 days of life run on styled capitals.

Kingdoms of the world in the smoke test: as the last thing of the first launch the operator calls a large kingdom of the world at -700 -700 (`admin npc spawn`) and the server stops right after it claims its land; the second launch checks that the appearance went on, ended without a second founding, the founding money came once (10000) and agrees with the treasury and no citizen came twice; earlier in the first launch the operator calls another large kingdom at 700 -700 and measures the ticks of its whole appearance (a worst tick over 50 ms fails; long ticks are logged with their stage). With `-Pack` the same in the biomes of the mods of the pack, with the wood of the place in the log.
Rulers in the smoke test: in the second launch `admin npc days <site> 10` runs ten turns of the kingdom that ended its appearance (the treasury agrees with the journal, the ruler lays out a building and trades at its market or in deals); the memory of the ruler of the first large kingdom is read before the stop and compared after the restart together with format 3; in the first launch six small kingdoms more appear and the ticks of a turn of all kingdoms of the world are measured (`admin npc turn all`): the average tick, the worst one, the worst without pauses of the garbage collector and the worst tick of a turn itself (the look at a kingdom, the decisions, the answers), which must stay within 50 ms; laying out a building goes through the shared path of construction (reservations of all constructions are counted again) and may take longer, which is reported as a warning. Unit tests of decisions from a snapshot are in `RulerMindTest` and `RulerMemoryTest`, game tests of rulers in `RulerGameTests` (a house when the beds are taken and the builders at work, land of others and land where players lived, a deal of a player, money and the journal, a decree in the chronicle).

The army in the smoke test: in the first launch two or three citizens become warriors and an archer and take the best weapons of the storehouse (with `-Pack` the best weapons of Simply Swords and of L'Ender's Cataclysm with Cataclysmic Combat, found with `admin army weapons`; the choice is logged), a squad holds the point 20 70 20, a band of mercenaries is hired for its advance from the treasury (the money of the currency falls by exactly the advance). In the second launch the squad with its order, the band with the day it is paid until and the soldiers with the same weapons are there, and dismissed soldiers bring exactly one of each of their items back to the storehouse; no money appeared.

Script options:

- `-Pack`: a server in `server-pack` with the server-side and common mods of the modpack (default `D:\Games\Minecraft\Instances\Taghi RPG & Create RU\mods`; the folder is only read). Client-only mods are skipped.
- `-SkipBuild`: use the jar that is already built.
- `-OldWorld <folder>`: one launch on a copy of a world saved by an early version of the mod instead of the two: every part of its data is read, nothing goes read-only, its kingdoms, citizens and money are there, and the old world keeps its plan of capitals without provinces, though Forge adds `plan = "states"` to its config.
- `-Load`: the load of a busy server is added to the second launch (`-LoadMinutes`, 30 minutes by default).
- `-States`: instead of the scenarios, a new world with the plan of states (`plan = "states"`): the first launch waits for the plan to be made and measures it (the time of every stage, the worst tick of the plan, the states founded and their provinces, the memory of the tiles, the size of the files), the second checks that the plan stays made and the provinces stay where they were. With `-OldWorld` the script writes `plan = "states"` into the config of the old world even where it says `legacy`, and the old world must still keep the plan it had, without provinces. The scenarios without `-States` give the new world `plan = "legacy"`.
- `-WithoutMod`: diagnostic run without KingdomWorld, to see whether a pack mod breaks the server on its own.
- `-ExcludeModIds a,b`: leave out additional mods by id.
- `-TestEnvRoot`, `-PackModsDir`, `-JavaHome`, `-Memory`: paths and server memory.
- `-MapModsDir <folder>`: also install the server map mods of the folder (BlueMap, Dynmap; other jars are skipped, the folder is only read). The script accepts the download of the game's resources in BlueMap's config (BlueMap does not start without it) and checks after the first start and after the restart that every map mod shows both kingdoms and the caravan: by the marker lines in the log, and for BlueMap also by its live markers over HTTP (`http://127.0.0.1:8100`).

Unit tests of land outlines check a rectangle, the letter L, a hole, enclaves and chunks touching at a corner, neighbouring kingdoms with a shared border, 200 random shapes (the centre of every chunk lies in exactly its area, and outside the land in none) and changes: claiming or losing a chunk redraws only its kingdom, losing a middle chunk leaves a hole, losing a bridge leaves two enclaves. The game test of maps checks that a client gets all land of its dimension, over the limit only the square around the player, that a kingdom hidden from the player reaches neither the snapshot nor the atlas, that the snapshot survives the trip and that the request goes through the request guard.

Unit tests of diplomacy check the bonds and their changes (peace, pact, alliance, vassal, war, truce), the bans on declaring war, the preparation and start of a war with the call of allies and vassals, score and exhaustion within their range, the white peace when the time runs out, terms of peace by the lead and their application together (vassalage, tribute, claims), the independence of a vassal, the shield of the absent (with its delay, not right after a fight), the decisions of rulers of the world by character and the limits on their wars, and plural forms. Game tests of war: a declaration, its preparation and start; soldiers attack an enemy player online and leave one away or offline alone; PvP between kingdoms at war goes through, between kingdoms at peace it does not; a raid takes goods from the cargo of a caravan without making any and pays the buyer back; peace with an indemnity moves money by the books; a kingdom with nobody online is shielded; the law does not judge the killing of an enemy; the data outlives a save. The smoke test declares two wars of kingdoms of the world and a war of the kingdom of players against a kingdom of the world, measures the ticks and steps of the wars, checks score and exhaustion after a restart and makes peace with an indemnity, checking the money.

Unit tests of the nobility check the stability and its causes, how often parties demand (played days only), answers and their consequences (a bargain halves both), a plot that ripens, is found out by counter-intelligence and overthrows a ruler of the world, a plot against a king of players (no overthrow: rebels keep income, after a warning a city leaves), nothing moving while nobody is online, the success, failure and capture of spies, theft from a treasury by the books, marriages of houses. Game tests (`NobilityGameTests`): a title makes a player noble with the share of the fief and no rights over the treasury; a demand and its answer move the stability; a plot changes the ruler of a kingdom of the world; a plot against a king of players takes a city into a kingdom of the world of its own without loss or duplication; a spy steals from a foreign treasury by the books; a caught spy spoils the relations; the nobility outlives a save.

The nobility in the smoke check: in the first launch `admin nobility tick` makes the courts of the kingdoms of the world, SmokeTest gets a house with the title of count on its east march, a plot, a demand and a spy sent to scout; `admin nobility day` runs the day of the courts one kingdom a tick and prints the average and worst tick; in the second launch, after the restart, the counts of houses, titles, demands, plots and spies and the money of SmokeTest add up.

Large states (stage 15). Unit tests of `core.land` check the survey of the land and the kinds of cells (`SurveyClassifierTest`), the bound of the radius and the size of the survey (`StateRulesTest`), the layout of states and provinces (`StatePlannerTest`, `ProvincePlannerTest`), the borders along water (`BorderRefinerTest`), the places of settlements, the choices of the world and the places near a place in the same province (`SettlementPlannerTest`, `SettlementPlacedTest`), the roads of the plan (`RoadPlannerTest`), the stages of the plan in order, a restart in every stage without work done twice and the budget of a tick (`PlanProgressTest`), the time of the plan of eight large states and the quota of 3 large and 5 small ones (`RealmPlanTimingTest`). Tests of `core.territory`: tiles to runs and back and a hundred thousand changes against a plain map, foreign land nearby, claims beside provinces and joined through them, the chunks of a province told to listeners a few thousand a tick, the provinces of a kingdom removed or not known (`ProvinceLandTest`), the neighbours of a state by provinces (`BordersTest`). Tests of `core.npc`: the price and the refusals of a sale (`ProvinceSaleTest`), the growth by provinces (`ProvinceGrowthTest`), folded citizens (`FoldedCitizenTest`), the guard of settlements (`SettlementGarrisonTest`), the layouts and tickets of settlements by rank and the same citizen for its slot (`SettlementLayoutsTest`), the life of settlements in numbers and a settlement handed over while it rises (`SettlementLifeTest`); the wilds (`WildsTest`); the roads of the plan: the search in a corridor, lines within a tolerance, the way a byte a block, the cache of the network, the tracks of the plan for caravans and the cells of cities, buildings and roads (`CorridorSearchTest`, `PolylinesTest`, `RoadCellsTest`, `RoadNetworkCacheTest`, `RoadNetworkTracksTest`, `CellIndexTest`); war: the holds of provinces, the battle with a garrison and the guards who serve in a settlement as soldiers (`ProvinceHoldsTest`), the provinces of a peace and settlements handed over by the journal (`ProvinceWarTest`), the pace of the journal (`ConquestPaceTest`), the treaty of cession (`CessionTest`); formats and packets (`SettlementDataTest`, `SiegeFormatTest`, `DiplomacyFormatTest`, `ProvincePacketsTest`, `MapSyncPacketsTest`), the outlines of provinces for web maps (`ProvinceOutlinesTest`) and files written off the thread (`AsyncFileWriterTest`). Game tests: `LandPlanGameTests` (a small ring of states is planned, founded and given its provinces; a plan stopped in the survey and in the provinces comes to the same end; a block of the noise that fails once is asked again, and one that always fails stops the plan where it is; a world that does not ask for states keeps the plan of capitals; a region file that cannot be read counts as generated all over; a read-only file of provinces keeps the plan of capitals waiting), `ProvinceGameTests` (a province guards the land of its holder, founding and claiming keep away from provinces, an annexation hands the provinces to the winner, provinces survive a save and a newer file is read-only), `ProvinceSaleGameTests` (a kingdom buys a province next to its land and the money moves in the books; a charter founds a kingdom on the province and pays the seller; a charter without a throne gives the money back; the heart, a province where people live and an enemy at war are refused; a ruler takes a wild province but not one next to a player), `LandRoadGameTests` (the wilds of a state are free and its settlements are not; the limits of cities and roads are for players only; a road of the plan is laid once between standing settlements), `SettlementGameTests` (a small ring gets its settlements by rank in its provinces; a plan stopped while placing its settlements comes to the same places; sixty days of the numbers touch no treasury and no storehouse; a village rises and goes on after a restart without copies; the citizens of a village fold and come back as themselves), `ProvinceWarGameTests` (an annexation of a state of 25 provinces with settlements, buildings and citizens, the worst second of the journal 15 ms at most; a restart in the middle of the journal neither loses nor doubles; far from players a squad beats the garrison and holds the square and another the stone; a ruler of the world cedes a bordering province for its price and refuses one to a kingdom it distrusts; a state disputes its land with its neighbour once no wild land is free), `MapGameTests` (provinces reach a client as it may see them), `PropertyIndexGameTests` (buildings, walls and roads are found as before the indexes), `ScaleGameTests` (three kingdoms of 1700 citizens step in slices; a writer stopped in the middle leaves the old file) and `IndexBenchGameTests` (a step of a kingdom of a thousand citizens measured). The check of words of war in the translation test covers the line above the hotbar of every place held, the sieges in the chronicle and the rumors of war. The smoke test with `-States` makes the plan of states on a new world and measures it.

### Running in development

- `gradlew runClient`: client with the mod (folder `run/`). `gradlew runClient -PquickPlayWorld=<world folder>` opens a singleplayer world right away.
- `gradlew runServer`: dedicated server with the mod (folder `run/`; accept the EULA in `run/eula.txt`).
- `-PwithMaps=journeymap`, `-PwithMaps=xaero` or `-PwithMaps=all` adds the client map mods to the development runs (downloaded from Modrinth; nothing of them goes into the jar, and the usual build and tests run without them). The JourneyMap API and the library of Xaero's maps are only published inside the mods, so the build takes them from there. The first build needs access to `repo.mikeprimm.com` (Dynmap API), `repo.bluecolored.de` (BlueMap API) and Modrinth.
- `-PwithMagic` adds Iron's Spells 'n Spellbooks and Ars Nouveau (with GeckoLib, Curios, Caelus and playerAnimator) to the development runs; their APIs are only needed at compile time, nothing of them goes into the jar.
- Maps self-check: `gradlew runClient -PwithMaps=journeymap -PclientSelfTest=maps -PquickPlayWorld=<world folder> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` founds two kingdoms in a singleplayer world (a square with a hole and one shaped like the letter L with a Cyrillic name), shows a caravan between them and, once the map mod has drawn the terrain, saves screenshots of the minimap and of the full screen map (with the pointer over the capital of the first kingdom and over the land of the second) in English and Russian, then dissolves the kingdoms and quits. `-PwithMaps=xaero` does the same for Xaero's maps.
- Client self-check: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=title` reaches the title screen and quits; `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=world -PquickPlayWorld=<world folder>` founds a kingdom next to the player in a singleplayer world, opens the map, the throne screen (through the real server packet) with all tabs, the territory editor with a claim, the post screen, the bank screen and the founding screen, then dissolves the kingdom and quits. Steps are logged with the prefix `KingdomWorld client self-test`. Without the `clientSelfTest` property this code does nothing.
- Siege showcase: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=siege -PquickPlayWorld=<copy of a test world> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` puts up a stone wall with a portcullis around a capital next to the player, declares a war of raiders for a city, sends six soldiers with a ram to the gate, marks a second city taken and some chunks occupied, and saves the closed gate, the siege of the gate with the line above the hotbar and the atlas into the folder `world`. Game tests of sieges: the land of an enemy is open in war but not of a third kingdom, not under the shield of the absent and never the throne; a chest is robbed only in war; gates close before enemies, open for their own and break to a soldier with a ram in the expected time; a square is held far from players, defenders interrupt it and the peace hands the city over; an annexation moves the treasury and the citizens without duplicates; builders mend a broken block of a wall from the storehouse after the war; the load of thirty against thirty. Smoke: a kingdom of the world besieges the city of SmokeTest, the square is held 40% over a restart, the peace for the city hands it over, citizens and cities add up.
- Layout check: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=layout -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` draws every screen of the mod off-screen in English and Russian, for windows of 854x480 and 1920x1080 and GUI scales 2, 3 and 4 (clamped as the game clamps them), and checks that texts fit their place and their buttons, nothing is cut off or leaves the screen, and lines above the hotbar fit the narrowest screen. Problems go to the log (language, window, scale, screen, translation key) and to `layout-check.txt`, and the client exits with code 1. Screenshots of scales 2 and 4 are saved to the folder. The screens size themselves from the width of the translated texts; long kingdom and player names are shortened with an ellipsis and shown in full on hover.
- Roads showcase: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=roads -PquickPlayWorld=<copy of a test world> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` flattens land with a stream next to the player, founds a kingdom with a storehouse of gravel and planks, lays a gravel road across the stream (with a bridge) paid from the storehouse, puts a merchant with llamas on the road and a camp of robbers beside it, then takes screenshots of the bridge, the caravan and the camp into the folder `world`.
- Background appearance check: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=background -PquickPlayWorld=<a new world>` (a folder with a `level.dat` alone) puts the player in creative mode in place and measures the ticks of the integrated server minute by minute until all kingdoms of the plan stand (or 25 minutes pass): as the game times them, whole, and as the game and the appearance made them, with the pauses of the garbage collector and without.
- Kingdom of the world showcase: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=npc -PquickPlayWorld=<copy of a test world> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` finds flat land near the player, calls a large kingdom of the world there (it appears as for any player, over ticks, with towns and roads), flies over it and saves screenshots of the capital, the square, the wall, a gate, the market, the road to a town, the town and the atlas into the folder `world`.
- Life showcase: `gradlew runClient -PclientSelfTest=life -PquickPlayWorld=<a copy of a test world> -PclientSelfTestScreenshots=<folder>` builds the tavern of the mod next to the player with an innkeeper, guests and food in a storehouse and three beds beside it, and in the evening shoots the guests in the tavern and the window of the tavern, at night the citizens asleep, into the folder `world`.

### Commands

For every player (`/kingdom`):

- `info [name]`: your kingdom, the kingdom whose land you stand on, or the named one.
- `list`: all kingdoms.
- `join <name>`: apply; `accept [name]` and `decline <name>`: answer an invitation.
- `leave`: leave your kingdom.
- `invite <player>`, `approve <player>`, `reject <player>`, `kick <player>`: for the recruiting right.
- `role <player> <position>`: appoint (`marshal`, `treasurer`, `chancellor`, `spymaster`, `court_mage`, `steward`, `subject`), king only.
- `abdicate <player>`: hand over the crown.
- `claim`, `unclaim`: claim or give up the chunk you stand in (territory right).
- `citizens`: the citizens of your kingdom (or of the land you stand on): population, beds, food, mood.
- `balance`: your accounts in every currency and your debts to treasuries.
- `pay <player> <amount> [kingdom]`, `pay confirm`: pay another player in the currency of your kingdom or the named one; large payments must be confirmed.
- `treasury`: the treasury of your kingdom; `treasury pay <player> <amount>`: pay from the treasury (Treasury right); `treasury deposit <amount> [kingdom]`: pay from your account into a treasury (debts first).
- `value [item]`: the worth of the item in your hand or the named one.
- `province price [id]`: the price of the province you stand in or of the one named, and why it is not for sale; `province buy <id>`: buy it for your kingdom (Treasury and Territory rights), or buy a charter when you are in no kingdom.
- `conquest swear|free`: after your kingdom was annexed, become a subject of the winner or stay free (three days).
- `dissolve`, then `dissolve confirm` within 30 seconds: dissolve the kingdom (king only).

Operators (permission level 2, `/kingdomworld`):

- `admin found <name> <x y z> [ruler]`: place a throne and found a kingdom (without a ruler from the console).
- `admin delete <name>`: delete a kingdom.
- `admin setruler <name> <player>`: set the ruler.
- `admin claim|unclaim <name> <chunkX> <chunkZ>`: claim or give up a chunk without rights or limit checks (the other rules apply).
- `admin owner <chunkX> <chunkZ>`: who owns a chunk.
- `admin bypass`: toggle protection bypass.
- `admin data status`: state of the data; `admin data unlock`: leave read-only mode (a backup is made first, then only what could be read is kept).
- `admin province fill <id> <kingdom|none> <x1> <z1> <x2> <z2>`: a rectangle of chunks into a province (up to 512 by 512 chunks at once), `admin province give <id> <kingdom|none>`: another holder, `admin province info [chunkX chunkZ]`: the province where one stands or of a chunk, `admin province clear <id>`: give a province up; `admin land info`: provinces, tiles and memory, `admin land unlock`: leave read-only mode of the province data and of the survey of the land (each backed up first), `admin land plan`: the stage of the plan of states, the work done and the time of every stage, `admin land survey`: how far the survey of the land has got and what it found.
- `admin citizens list [kingdom]`: citizens (short id, name, profession, state, health, home) and how many are in the world now.
- `admin citizens spawn <count> [kingdom]`: add citizens regardless of the growth rules (next to you, or at the throne from the console).
- `admin citizens kill <id>`: the citizen dies (with a chronicle entry); `admin citizens info <id>`: everything about a citizen; `admin citizens profession <id> <profession>`: give a profession; `admin citizens body <id> <x y z> [still]`: give a citizen a body at a place even with no players near (`still`: the body only stands and sees), `admin citizens body <id> remove`: return the citizen to the usual rules of bodies (for checks on a server without players). The start of the id is enough.
- `admin posts list [kingdom]`: work posts (place, profession, workers, radius, state, gathered today, yield); `admin posts set <x y z> profession|radius|seats|herd|animal|auto <value>`: set up a post without checking rights.
- `admin orders list [kingdom]`: orders with their sub-orders and what is missing; `admin orders add <item> <count> keep|make [kingdom]`: give an order; `admin orders cancel <number> [kingdom]`: cancel it.
- `admin recipes list <profession> [filter]`: what a workshop makes (the filter `mods` keeps only items of mods, any other filter matches part of the id); `admin recipes stock <item> [kingdom]`: put the ingredients of one making into the storehouses.
- `admin storage put <item> <count> [kingdom]`: put items into the storehouses; `admin storage list [kingdom]`: what the storehouses hold.
- `admin economy give|take <player> <amount> [kingdom]`: add or take money on an account; `admin economy treasury <kingdom> add|take <amount>`: change a treasury (journaled as admin); `admin economy payday <kingdom>`: hold the payday now; `admin economy info <kingdom>`: the treasury with exact numbers; `admin economy accounts <player>`: the accounts of a player; `admin economy values`: work out item values again and show the totals; `admin economy value <item>`: the worth of an item.
- `admin trade sell <kingdom> <item> <price> <keep>`, `admin trade buy <kingdom> <item> <price> <up to> <budget>`, `admin trade export <kingdom> <item> <above>`, `admin trade import <kingdom> <item> <count>`: the market of a kingdom (a price of 0 is the auto price, a budget of 0 has no limit); `admin trade propose <seller> <buyer> <item> <count> <price> [days]`: propose a deal for the seller (the price in the buyer's currency), `admin trade accept <kingdom> <number>`: accept it; `admin trade deals`, `admin trade caravans`: deals and caravans with exact numbers; `admin trade arrive`: every caravan arrives now without the chance of loss; `admin trade wallet <kingdom> <name> <amount>` and `admin trade purchase <kingdom> <name> <item> <count>`: money for a test buyer and a purchase at the market from its account; `admin trade rates`: rates, the money of currencies, coins in hands, treasuries and reserves.
- `admin guild rank <player> <rank>`, `admin guild points <player> <amount>`, `admin guild info <player>`, `admin guild join <player> <kingdom>`: rank, points and membership in the guild; `admin guild crystal <player> <rank> [count]`: give crystals; `admin guild contract list [kingdom]`, `admin guild contract post <kingdom> hunt|gather|bounty <monster, item or rank> <count> <reward> [days]` (ids with a colon in quotes: `"minecraft:zombie"`), `admin guild contract take <player> <id>`, `admin guild contract kill <player> <monster> [count]`, `admin guild contract expire <id>`, `admin guild contract cancel <id>`: contracts (the kingdom posts them from its treasury); `admin guild hire <kingdom> <count>`: citizens without work become adventurers; `admin guild branch <kingdom> [rank]`: the rank of the branch, the contracts finished there and the head of the guild, or a new rank (a branch of players keeps it within the limit of its guild house); `admin guild squad list [kingdom]`, `admin guild squad form <kingdom> <size>`, `admin guild squad send <squad> <contract>`, `admin guild squad return`: squads and expeditions (bringing them back at once); `admin guild monsters [filter]`: ranks of the monsters of the game and the mods; `admin guild money`: money in the guild's escrow; `admin guild energy <kingdom>`: crystals and energy; `admin guild status`, `admin guild unlock`: the state of the guild's data and leaving read-only mode.
- `admin build place <kingdom> <template> <x y z> <turn> <mirror> <wood> [stone]`: a construction of other wood and stone (`"minecraft:spruce"`, `auto` for the wood of the place, `none` as drawn); `admin build priority <number> low|normal|high`: the priority of a construction; `admin build materials`: the woods and stones of the server; `admin build role <building>`: role, its state, level, integrity, beds and residents, numbers, materials, costs of an upgrade and a repair; `admin build upgrade|repair|demolish <building>`: order the work; `admin build autorepair <building> true|false`; `admin build check <building>`: look at the building now; `admin build damage <building> <blocks>`: take blocks away from the top without drops (for checks); `admin build blueprints [kingdom]`: own blueprints; `admin build capture <kingdom> <x y z> <x y z> <name> [role] [category]`: save a building as a blueprint.
- `admin build templates`: building templates, their size, blocks and items and whether they can be built; `admin build list [kingdom]`, `admin build buildings [kingdom]`: constructions and the register with exact numbers; `admin build place <kingdom> <template> <x y z> [rotation 0-3] [mirror]`: lay out a construction; `admin build info <id>`: steps, placed and paid blocks, what is still to pay, what lies at the site and what is set aside; `admin build stock <id>`: put what a construction still needs into the storehouses; `admin build finish <id>`: build it at once without materials (the register remembers the operator); `admin build cancel <id>`: cancel it; `admin build pause <id>`, `admin build resume <id>`: pause it and let it go on; `admin build tick`: place paid blocks and refresh states now.
- `admin army status`, `admin army unlock`: the state of the army's data and leaving read-only mode; `admin army recruit <kingdom> <count> [warrior|archer]`: unemployed citizens become soldiers and take their gear; `admin army dismiss <kingdom>`: dismiss every soldier; `admin army branch <kingdom> guard|army <count>`: move fighters to the guard or to the army; `admin army tactics`: the squads fighting by the plans of their leaders now (shape, roles) and the time of the decisions; `admin army garrison <kingdom>`: a garrison in one line (the guard, the army, the officers, how many soldiers stand at every post, the steps of the alarms, where the throne stands); `admin army staff <kingdom>`: see to the staff now, as a kingdom of the world does once a day (replace the fallen, fill the posts of officers, issue the gear from the treasury); `admin army soldiers <kingdom>` and `admin army gear <kingdom>`: soldiers with their weapons and armor (the second refreshes the gear first); `admin army weapons <mod>`: the weapons of a mod as soldiers judge them; `admin army squad list [kingdom]`, `admin army squad form <kingdom> <size>`, `admin army squad disband <squad>`: squads; `admin army order <squad> none|hold <x y z>|patrol <x y z>|guard <x y z> <radius>|follow <player>|escort <deal>`: orders; `admin army mercenary offers <kingdom> [new]`, `admin army mercenary hire <kingdom> <band>`, `admin army mercenary release <band>`, `admin army mercenary list [kingdom]`: mercenaries; `admin army battle <squad> <monsters> [strength]`: an abstract battle for checks.
- `admin companions status`, `admin companions unlock`: the state of the companion data and leaving read-only mode; `admin companions offer <kingdom> [refresh]`: the offers of a market (new ones at once); `admin companions give <player> [level] [kingdom]`, `fund <player> <kingdom> <amount>`, `buy <player> <kingdom>`: a companion, money on the account, buying the first slave by the rules of the market; `pack <player> <item> <count>`, `gear <player>`, `list <player>`, `loyalty <player> <0-100>`, `hunger <player> <0-20>`: the pack, the gear, the list, loyalty and food of companions; `market <kingdom> <true|false>`: close or open the slave market; `party add <leader> <member>`, `party fire <leader> <true|false>`, `party disband <leader>`: parties. A player is given by UUID or by name, offline too.
- `admin law status`, `admin law unlock`: the state of the law data and leaving read-only mode; `admin law wanted [kingdom]`: the wanted; `admin law wanted add <player> <kingdom> theft|assault|murder|trespass|vandalism [worth]`: a crime seen by guards; `admin law report`: deliver the reports now; `admin law pardon <player> <kingdom>`; `admin law fine <player> <kingdom> pay|set <amount>`: take the fine (coins, account, debt) or set it; `admin law execute <player> <kingdom> [kit]`: an execution (a player who is offline is replaced by a stand-in at the throne, `kit` gives it a sword, bread and a chestplate); `admin law confiscated [player]`, `confiscated ransom <id>`, `confiscated store <id>`: confiscated goods, the ransom from the owner's account at the throne, sending them to the storehouses; `admin law reputation <player> <kingdom> [-100..100]`; `admin law debt <player> <kingdom> [add <amount>]`; `admin law pass <player> <kingdom> [grant|revoke]`; `admin law gate <player> <kingdom>`: a player online comes to the gate of a large city; `admin law court <kingdom> [multiplier|fee|bounty|slavery <value>]`: the laws of the court; `admin law act <player> <kingdom> at <x y z>|use <x y z>|break <x y z>|strike <citizen id>|leave`: the acts of a stand-in for a player who is offline, by the same events a real player raises (`at` puts it at a place, `use` clicks a block with the right button and takes one item if a window of a container opens, `break` breaks a block as in survival mode (a kingdom of players counts as present for that moment: the law judges damaged property only while somebody is there to answer), `strike` hits the body of a citizen, `leave` lets it go; for checks of the law on a server without players); `admin law alarms <kingdom>`: the alarms of a kingdom, whether the guards saw the offender and how far the nearest soldier is from it; `admin law damage <kingdom>`: how many blocks damaged in peace wait to be put back. A player is given by UUID or name, also when offline.
- `/kingdom guide`: the book "Kingdoms: a Guide" when you carry none.
- `/kingdom law`: the window of the law.
- `/kingdom party`: the party window; `/kingdom party invite|kick|leader <player>`, `accept`, `decline`, `leave`, `fire <true|false>`, `say <text>`; `/party <text>`: party chat.
- `admin magic energy <kingdom> [add|set <amount>]`, `admin magic crystals <kingdom> <rank> <count>`, `admin magic dissolve <kingdom> <count>`: the energy of a kingdom (store, capacity, crystals, research, spells, devices), crystals into the storehouses and dissolving the crystals of the storehouses; `admin magic research <kingdom> <research> done|undo|start`, `admin magic spell <kingdom> <spell> on|off`, `admin magic ward <kingdom> <chunkX> <chunkZ>`: the tower and the spells of the kingdom; `admin magic learn <player> <spell> [forget]`, `admin magic staff <player>`, `admin magic cast <player> <spell>`: magic of players; `admin magic wells <kingdom> <pos>`: a pulse of the wells of other mods with a stand-in player, totals to the log; `admin magic status|unlock`: the state of the magic data.
- `admin roads status`, `admin roads unlock`: the state of the road data and leaving read-only mode; `admin roads city list`, `admin roads city add <kingdom> <x y z> [name]` (places a town hall), `admin roads city remove <id>`, `admin roads city level <kingdom> <city|0>` (level, citizens, buildings, gates): cities; `admin roads wall list`, `admin roads wall build <kingdom> <city|0> palisade|stone`, `admin roads wall gate <id> <x y z>`, `admin roads wall pay <id>`, `admin roads wall place <id>`, `admin roads wall remove <id>`: walls; `admin roads road list`, `admin roads road plan <kingdom> <from x y z> <to x y z> path|gravel|paved [joint|payer]` (searches the way at once), `admin roads road pay <id>` (pay for all of it from the storehouses of its builders), `admin roads road place <id>` (place the paid blocks, loading their chunks), `admin roads road wear <id> <0-1>`, `admin roads road answer <request> accept|decline`, `admin roads road remove <id>`: roads; `admin roads camp list`, `admin roads camp add <x y z> [robbers]`, `admin roads camp grow <id>`, `admin roads camp remove <id>`: camps; `admin roads ambush <caravan>`: the nearest camp falls upon a caravan now; `admin roads caravans`: caravans with their way through the roads; `admin roads gear`: the weapons and armor robbers take.
- `admin npc status`, `admin npc unlock`: the state of the data of the kingdoms of the world and leaving read-only mode; `admin npc plan`: the plan (places, how many wait for players, whether it is made); `admin npc list`: every place with its size, towns, state, stage, kingdom and ruler, and for a kingdom that stands its treasury, founding money, citizens, soldiers, buildings and towns; `admin npc spawn here [small|medium|large]`, `admin npc spawn <x> <z> [size]`: a kingdom of the world appears on flat land near there (within 256 blocks) for checks; `admin npc spawn site <id>`: a planned place appears now; `admin npc spawn background <id>`: the buildings of a founded place are raised in the background with the small budget, players or not (for measuring); `admin npc remove <id> [buildings]`: the kingdom of a place goes with its land, citizens and treasury, with `buildings` also its buildings, throne, town halls and wall; `admin npc reset-plan`: the places still waiting are forgotten and the plan is made again; `admin npc timing`: the ticks of the last appearance (count, average and worst); `admin npc settlement info <id>`: a settlement of a state (people, how many are named and folded, houses, food, guard, militia), `admin npc settlement raise <id>`: raise a waiting settlement now with the full budget, `admin npc settlement fold|unfold <id>`: fold its citizens into numbers or bring them back.
- `admin life status`, `admin life unlock`: the time of day, the ill, rooms, goods of fairs and leaving read-only mode; `admin life time morning|day|evening|night`: set the time to the middle of that part of the day of citizens; `admin life tavern <kingdom>`: taverns, whether open, the guests of the evening; `admin life rumors <kingdom> [refresh]`: the rumors of the taverns today (with the number of a camp); `admin life sick list <kingdom>`, `admin life sick <citizen> [heal]`: illnesses; `admin life festival <kingdom>`: a festival as the king calls it; `admin life fair <kingdom> [city]`: open a fair now.
- `admin war status`, `admin war unlock`: the state of the diplomacy data and leaving read-only mode; `admin war list`: wars (score, exhaustion, battles, raids, blockades, losses, shields) and treaties; `admin war declare <attacker> <defender> <goal> [break|now]`: a war by an operator (the defender may have nobody online, `break` breaks a treaty, `now` starts at once); `admin war start <war>`; `admin war peace <war> [winner [indemnity]]`: a white peace, the goal of the winner in full or an indemnity, money checked; `admin war score <war> [attacker|defender <0-100>]`, `admin war exhaustion <war> attacker|defender <0-100>`; `admin war relation <first> <second> [reason]`; `admin war truce <first> <second> <days>`, `admin war treaty <first> <second> pact|alliance|vassal|truce`; `admin war present <kingdom> on|off|auto`: count the players of a kingdom as online or not (for checks of the shield); `admin war tick [steps]`: steps of the wars now with their timing, `admin war tick day`: a day of the wars now; `admin war timing`: ticks of diplomacy, average and worst.
- `admin npc turn <site>`: a turn of the ruler now (decisions, answers to deals, needs); `admin npc turn all`: a turn of every ruler in turn with the ticks measured; `admin npc days <site> <n>`: n turns in a row with their decisions carried out (the treasury before and after, checked against the journal); `admin npc decisions <site>`: decrees and decisions waiting; `admin npc needs <site>`: needs, relations to neighbours and the times of turns. `admin npc timing` shows the ticks of rulers too.
- `admin simulate <kingdom> [steps]`: run steps of the citizen simulation at once (for tests).
- `debug create|list|info|remove`: the stage 0 debug commands.

Quote kingdom names with spaces in admin commands: `"Old Valoria"`.

### Configuration

- `<world>/serverconfig/kingdomworld-server.toml` (per world):
  - `npc`: `maxActiveNpcs` (300, citizen bodies per server), `citizenActivationChunks` (6);
  - `guild`: `healthWeight` (1), `damageWeight` (4), `armorWeight` (2), `speedWeight` (20), `monsterRankScores` (0, 25, 45, 70, 120, 250, 500, 1000, 2000), `bossRankBonus` (2), `bossMinRank` (`a`), `monsterRanks`, `crystalEnergy` (10 ... 100000), `crystalChance` (0.05 ... 1), `crystalValue` (2 ... 20000), `farmWindowMinutes` (10), `farmFreeKills` (12), `farmHalfKills` (8), `spawnerFactor` (0.5), `rankPoints` (0, 30, 100, 300, 800, 2000, 5000, 12000, 30000), `killPoints` (1 ... 256), `examKills` (5, 5, 4, 3, 1, ...), `examMinutes` (60), `examCooldownMinutes` (20), `penaltyPercent` (50), `joinFee` (0), `maxActiveContracts` (3), `maxContractsPerBranch` (32), `maxPostedPerPlayer` (5), `minCommissionPercent` (0), `maxCommissionPercent` (30), `defaultCommissionPercent` (10), `postingFeePermille` (20), `maxContractDays` (7), `defaultDailyBudget` (50), `maxDailyBudget` (100000), `autoRewardPerKill` (1 ... 1500), `maxAutoContracts` (3), `minSquadSize` (2), `maxSquadSize` (5), `expeditionBaseMinutes` (10), `expeditionMinutesPerRank` (5), `expeditionInjury` (0.25), `expeditionDeath` (0.04); tables by rank hold nine numbers for F...SSS;
  - `law`: `witnessRadius` (16), `reportMinSeconds` (10), `reportMaxSeconds` (30), `pettyDays` (3), `theftFinePercent` (200), `assaultFine` (50), `trespassFine` (30), `murderFine` (1000), `mergeSeconds` (60), `bountyReward` (200), `bountyDays` (3), `ransomPercent` (50), `confiscationDays` (7), `debtLimit` (500), `hailSeconds` (5), `beatenHealth` (2), `beatenWeaknessSeconds` (10), `theftReputation` (-5), `assaultReputation` (-8), `murderReputation` (-40), `trespassReputation` (-3), `tradePerPoint` (500), `taxReputation` (1), `contractReputation` (2), `highReputation` (50), `priceEffectPercent` (10), `slaveryFine` (300), `maxEntryFee` (10000), `maxBounty` (1000000), `slaveryWarnBlocks` (32), `fearSeconds` (18), `fearSightRadius` (16), `fearHearRadius` (10), `fearSpreadRadius` (5), `alarmSeconds` (60), `alarmRadius` (64), `ongoingSeconds` (10), `pursueCreative` (`true`), `vandalismFinePercent` (200), `vandalismReputation` (-5), `restoreQuietSeconds` (30), `treasuryFinePercent` (150), `storehouseWatchBlocks` (8);
  - `simulation`: `simulationIntervalTicks` (1200, the step of the abstract citizen simulation);
  - `citizens`: `growthFoodDays` (3), `growthIntervalMinutes` (10), `starvationCanKill` (`false`), `dailyNutrition` (6), `infirmaryCapacity` (4), `woundedHealthPercent` (50), `maxCitizensPerKingdom` (200), `farmRadius` (8);
  - `work`: `workPostDefaultRadius` (8), `workPostMaxRadius` (16), `workPostMaxSeats` (4), `maxTreeBlocks` (160), `mineLevels` (4), `maxHerdSize` (12), `carrierRange` (48 blocks), `carrierItemsPerMinute` (64), `taskRiskMultiplier` (1.0; 0 turns accidents far from players off), `professionRisks` (risk per minute of work as `profession:injury:death`, for example `miner:0.004:0.0004`);
  - `production`: `craftSeconds` (10, hand work on one thing for a beginner), `cookFoodDaysTarget` (5), `orderChainDepth` (3), `maxOrdersPerKingdom` (32);
  - `economy`: `silverValue` (10) and `goldValue` (100) of new currencies, `defaultPollTax` (10) and `maxPollTax` (1000), `maxWage` (200), `wages` (usual wages by profession, `profession:amount`), `moodWorkEffect` (0.2), `payConfirmAbove` (1000), `mintToVault` (`true`), `mintRates` (`item=coin:count` or `#tag=coin:count`), `mintSeconds` (15), `itemValues` (`item=value` or `#tag=value`), `defaultItemValue` (5), `laborMarkup` (0.1), `householdSharePercent` (20), `householdCoinsMax` (300), `householdFood` (6);
  - `trade`: `exportFactor` (0.6) and `importFactor` (1.5), `exportPerMerchant` (64), `exportExperienceBonus` (1.0), `exportMaxIncomePerDay` (2000), `importMaxPerDay` (256), `importDelayMinutes` (5), `importDenied` (ids and `#tags`), `demandElasticity` (0.5), `minPricePercent` (50) and `maxPricePercent` (300), `priceStockTarget` (64), `spreadPercent` (30), `defaultSalesTaxPercent` (5) and `maxSalesTaxPercent` (25), `maxLotsPerPlayer` (8), `maxMarketEntries` (32), `defaultExchangeFeePercent` (2) and `maxExchangeFeePercent` (10), `minRate` (0.1) and `maxRate` (10), `rateSmoothingPercent` (25), `rateProductionDays` (7), `rateMinSupply` (1000), `caravanBlocksPerMinute` (200), `caravanLossChance` (0.02), `proposalDays` (3), `maxDealsPerKingdom` (16), `maxDealPeriodDays` (30);
  - `player`: `afkTimeoutSeconds`, `combatTagSeconds` (placeholders for later stages);
  - `kingdom`: `allowedDimensions` (`["minecraft:overworld"]`), `minChunksFromOtherKingdom` (4), `initialClaimRadius` (1), `styleChangeCost` (2000, copper coins the treasury pays when the king changes the architectural style on the throne; 0 makes it free);
  - `territory`: `baseMaxChunks` (25), `chunksPerMember` (4), `chunksPerCitizen` (2 chunks for every living citizen, 0 turns it off), `claimBufferChunks` (1; 0 lets borders touch);
  - `protection`: `protectInteractions`, `protectFromExplosions`, `protectFromFire`, `allowFakePlayers` (all `true`), `peaceBreaking` (`crime` by default: blocks on the land of others can be broken in peace, in a city that is damaged property; `forbidden`: they cannot, as before version 1.3);
  - `roads`: `maxCities` (6), `minCityDistance` (160), `maxRoads` (32), `npcMaxRoads` (200: the roads of a kingdom of the world, whose settlements its plan gave and `maxCities` does not bound), `maxRouteLength` (1536), `searchStepsPerTick` (3000), `maxSearchSteps` (400000), `searchChunksPerTick` (6), `maxBridge` (32), `maxFill` (3), `secondsPerBlock` ([1, 3, 5]), `wearPerDay` ([0.004, 0.002, 0.001]), `repairShare` (0.25), `autoRepair` (`true`), `repairBelow` (0.5), `rememberConsent` (`true`), `townPeople` (12), `townBuildings` (6), `cityPeople` (40), `cityBuildings` (20), `wallRadius` (24), `caravanSpeed` ([1.6, 2.5, 3.5]), `speedOnRoads` (`true`), `playerSpeed` and `horseSpeed` ([0.1, 0.2, 0.3]), `crossingDays` (3), `physicalCaravans` (`true`), `bandits` (`true`), `maxCamps` (16 in new configs, older ones keep 8), `campMinDistance` (192), `campSpawnChance` (0.2), `campStartBandits` (3), `campMaxBandits` (12), `campGrowthChance` (0.35), `banditStrength` (18), `leaderStrength` (60), `ambushChance` (0.03), `campReach` (320), `ambushByClass` ([0.8, 0.6, 0.45]), `garrisonFactor` (0.35), `patrolFactor` (0.5), `ownLandFactor` (0.6), `protectRadius` (96), `raidParty` (6), `unguardedLoot` (0.75), `merchantDeath` (0.25), `maxPhysicalBandits` (8), `banditWeapons`, `banditArmor`; values by class in the order path, gravel, paved.
  - `npcKingdoms`: `generate` (`true`), `plan` (`states`: states with provinces; `legacy`: capitals as before; acting only on a new world, an old world keeps its capitals), `count` (8), `radius` (7000 in new configs, older ones keep theirs; the plan of states takes at most 20000), `minDistance` (1200), `minSpawnDistance` (1000), `triggerChunks` (12), `sizeQuota` ([5, 0, 3]), `sizeWeights` ([5, 3, 2]), `landRadius` ([4, 6, 7]), `towns` ([2, 3, 4]), `townDistance` (260), `startingMoney` ([6000, 15000, 40000]), `startingFoodDays` (10), `startingMaterials` ([6, 10, 16]), `soldiers` ([2, 4, 8]), `blocksPerTick` (2000), `chunksPerTick` (4), `maxInhabitedMinutes` (20); for the plan of states `stateChunks` ([9000, 13000, 16000, 22000, 28000, 36000], the least and the most land of a small, a medium and a large state in chunks), `provinceChunks` (1600), `gapBlocks` (384), `spawnZone` (1500), `neutralShare` (0.35); for the settlements of states `settlementTriggerChunks` ([16, 14, 12, 10], the chunks to a player that raise a large city, a small town, a village and a hamlet), `backgroundTiers` (`["city", "town"]`, the ranks the background raises near players), `backgroundSettlementBlocks` (2000), `maxNamedCitizens` (5000, from 100 to 100000), `settlerKeepDays` (2, from 1 to 60); values by size in the order small, medium, large; a capital of districts takes at least 4, 5 and 7 chunks of land whatever `landRadius` says, a styled capital at least 6, 7 and 8 (the capitals of states are of districts).
  - `land`: `wildernessOpen` (`true`: the wilds of kingdoms of the world are as free to players as land of nobody), `provincePricePerChunk` (3.0, from 0.5 to 100: the price of a chunk of a province on sale before the character of the ruler and the relation; a province costs at least 1500);
  - `siege`: `woodGateStrength` (720), `ironGateStrength` (1800), `occupyMinutes` (3: a claimed chunk, and an empty province at its boundary stone), `plazaMinutes` (5: the square of a city, and a province where people live), `throneMinutes` (5), `repairQuietMinutes` (10), `explosions` (`true`), `maxBodies` (60);
  - `life`: `workStart` (1000), `eveningStart` (11000), `sleepStart` (14500), `wakeUp` (23200), `spreadTicks` (700), `tavernShare` (50), `tavernMood` (8), `tavernMoodDays` (2), `sickChance` (15), `sickFactor` (2.0), `sickMood` (10), `sickWorkPercent` (50), `infirmaryDays` (1.0), `homeDays` (3.0), `festivalPerCitizen` (20), `festivalFood` (6), `festivalMood` (15), `festivalMoodDays` (3), `festivalEveryDays` (7), `daysPerYear` (96), `fairEveryDays` (7), `fairLevel` (`city`), `fairSeats` (2), `fairDiscount` (100), `fairVisitorItems` (64), `roomPrice` (30), `rumorPrice` (15), `rumorsMin` (2), `rumorsMax` (4); times in ticks of the day, money in copper coins of the kingdom.
  - `npcKingdoms.rulers`: `turnEveryDays` (1), `decisionsPerTurn` (3), `decreesPerDay` (2), `maxChunks` ([224, 352, 640]), `chunksPerClaim` (4), `freeBeds` (1), `storageFullPercent` (85), `roles` (storehouse, market, well, workshop, barracks, watchtower, tavern, mage tower by level), `townPopulation` (40), `maxTowns` (6), `reserveDays` (5), `soldiersPermille` (100), `slackPermille` (100), `roadDistance` (1200), `festivalMood` (35), `growthChunks` (2).
  - `war`: `preparationMinutes` (20), `maxWarDays` (7), `pactDays` (10), `truceDays` (10), `proposalMinutes` (20), `stepSeconds` (60), `battleChance` (0.35), `raidChance` (0.25), `exhaustionPerDay` (8), `imposeLead` (40), `tributePermille` (200), `tributeDays` (5), `vassalPermille` (100), `indemnityPermille` (300), `absenceMinutes` (5), `fightGraceSeconds` (60), `protectPeacefulPlayers` (`true`), `npcWarDays` (10), `npcRecentWarDays` (5), `npcWarsOnPlayers` (`true`), `npcPlayerWarDays` (20);
  - `maps`: `snapshotMaxChunks` (32768, chunks of land a client map gets at once) and `snapshotRadiusChunks` (64, the square around the player when there is more), `blueMap` and `dynmap` (`true`), `webMapCaravans` (`true`), `webMapFillOpacity` (0.3), `webMapBorderWidth` (2), `webMapLanguage` (`en_us`), `webMapUpdateSeconds` (5).
- `config/kingdomworld-common.toml`: `disabledCompatModules`, mod ids whose compat modules stay off.
- `config/kingdomworld-client.toml`: `requestKingdomListOnJoin`, `showBorderMessages` (messages when crossing borders), `showPartyList` (the list of the party in the corner); `maps`: `journeyMap` and `xaeroMaps` (`true`), `mapCaravans` (`true`), `mapFillOpacity` (0.25), `mapBorderWidth` (2.0).

### Data and data safety

Every system keeps its data in a file of its own, `<world>/data/kingdomworld_<name>.dat`, with a format number of its own:

| File | Format | What it holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `kingdoms` | 9 | kingdoms, land, citizens, storehouses, treasuries, markets, constructions and buildings |
| `households` | 1 | money set aside for the houses of citizens |
| `guild` | 1 | the adventurers' guild |
| `army` | 3 | armies |
| `trophies` | no number | trophies of war: the gear of the fallen until it is bought back |
| `companions` | 1 | companions and parties of players |
| `law` | 2 | the law |
| `magic` | 1 | magic |
| `roads` | 2 | cities, roads, walls, camps and caravans |
| `npc` | 6 | kingdoms of the world, their plan and the settlements of states |
| `life` | 1 | the life of cities |
| `diplomacy` | 2 | diplomacy and wars |
| `nobility` | 1 | the nobility |
| `siege` | 3 | war in the world |
| `provinces` | 1 | provinces and the plan of states |
| `survey` | 1 | the survey of the land of the plan of states |

Older formats are read as they are or upgraded on load; a format newer than this version understands keeps its data read-only. Network protocol 40: client and server must run the same version of the mod.

Kingdom data is stored in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_kingdoms.dat`, format 9 (roles and levels of the buildings of the register, their wood and stone, the blocks found missing and Repairs itself; the kinds of constructions (a new building, an upgrade, a repair, a demolition) with their building, the blocks they skip and what a demolition took down; the blueprints of kingdoms; constructions with their template, place, turn, priority, steps done and paid, materials at the site and orders, the register of buildings with integrity; the market of every kingdom with its lists, lots and orders from abroad, deals, caravans on the way and goods waiting for players; reserves of other currencies in treasuries, exchange rates, payments of deals in escrow and coins in hands; the treasury of every kingdom with its currency, books, vault of coins, journal, tax, wages and debts, the accounts of players, old currencies, the pay of every citizen on the last payday; kingdoms with land, members, rights, applications and invitations, citizens with their beds, workplaces and the state of their work, the chronicle, work posts with their settings, mode, workstations and yields, the record of production, orders, the contents of every storehouse and the gear of citizens). Data of formats 1-8 is upgraded automatically; the upgrade from format 2 gives the steward the citizens right, the upgrade from format 3 adds empty posts, records and gear, the upgrade from format 4 gives the steward the orders right, posts work on their own and there are no orders yet; the upgrade from format 5 gives the treasurer the Treasury right and kingdoms an empty treasury with the default currency whose first payday only starts the count, and nobody has an account yet; the upgrade from format 6 gives the treasurer and the steward the Market right and the treasurer and the chancellor the right of deals with kingdoms, there are no markets, deals or reserves yet and every currency starts at the rate of one; the upgrade from format 7 gives the steward the Construction right, there are no constructions or buildings yet; the upgrade from format 8 gives every building of the register the role its type names (if there is one) and level 1, constructions build new buildings, no kingdom has own blueprints, and no right changes; nothing else changes. Reservations of ingredients are not saved: after a restart nothing is set aside. Since stage 14d a kingdom keeps its architectural style in the optional key `style` (written once chosen; a style this version does not know is read as none) and a construction keeps the look it was laid out with; the format number stays 9.

The guild is stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_guild.dat`, format 1: members with ranks and points, the adventurers among the citizens, branches (commission, budget), contracts with their escrow and squads (also those away, with the time they come back). Loading the guild never throws either: data of a newer version, a damaged version or an unreadable entry keep the guild read-only (`/kingdomworld admin guild unlock`, with a backup). The marshal gets the right "Adventurers' guild" once: the kingdom data remembers the upgrades done in the list `upgrades`, without a new format number.

The army is stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_army.dat`, format 3 (it added the branch and the post of a soldier, the count of the fallen by branch, the vacant posts of officers, the day the staff was last seen to and the post of the garrison a squad stands at; in data of earlier formats the soldiers whose squad holds a point, guards an area or walks a patrol become the guard, the others the army, and all of them are fighters; a soldier with a branch or a post this version does not know is read as a fighter of the guard with a warning in the log, and the army is not shut for it): soldiers (branch, post, experience, kills, battles), squads with their commander and order, bands of mercenaries on offer and hired with the day they are paid until, barracks and the day of the last offers. The gear of soldiers is kept as the gear of citizens; the full health of a soldier is kept in the record of the citizen (`maxHealth`, only when it is not 20). Loading the army never throws: data of a newer format, a damaged version, an unreadable entry or a squad without its soldiers or band keep the army read-only (`/kingdomworld admin army unlock` with a backup). The marshal gets the "Army" right once (the upgrade `army_rights` in the list `upgrades`; the format of the kingdoms does not change).

Companions and parties are stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_companions.dat`, format 1: the records of companions with their packs, loyalty, hunger, wages and orders, the offers of the markets of the branches, closed markets, parties with their shares and contracts, invitations. Anything not understood (an item of a removed mod in a pack included) keeps the companion data read-only: nothing is lost and the file is not overwritten, and `/kingdomworld admin companions unlock` keeps what could be read after backing up the original.

The law is stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_law.dat`, format 2 (it added the crime of damaged property and the victim in a record: a citizen by the seed of its name, or the treasury; format 1 reads as it is, a theft without a victim stays a common theft): the wanted with their crimes, the decisions of the court and the contracts for heads, confiscated goods with the goods themselves, reputations and the trade that counts toward them, passes into cities, the laws of the courts and who pays entry by themselves; debts live in the treasuries. Anything not understood (an item of a mod that is gone among confiscated goods too) keeps the law read-only: nothing is lost and the file is not overwritten, `/kingdomworld admin law unlock` keeps what was read after a copy of the original. The chancellor of an old world gets the "Court" right once (upgrade `law_rights`, the format number of the kingdoms stays). Reports on the way and alarms live in memory only: a restart forgets them, the records stay. Money set aside for the houses of citizens from their wages until a house is loaded lies in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_households.dat` (format 1); the file is not critical: a record that cannot be read is simply gone, as the whole wage was before.

Magic is stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_magic.dat`, format 1: the energy of the kingdoms with the journal, research, the spells switched on and what they were paid for, the chunks of the ward, the blocks of magic on kingdom land (foci, accumulators, generators, gates) and the spells players learned. The energy left in started crystals travels with the crystals. Data that cannot be read or is newer is loaded read-only (changes are refused, the file is not overwritten) until `/kingdomworld admin magic unlock`. The court mage of older kingdoms gets the right `manage_magic` once on load (the upgrade `magic_rights`).

Roads are stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_roads.dat`, format 2 (the way of a road a byte a block, a road of 1000 blocks about 1 KB; format 1 is read as it is; the file is written off the server thread): cities, roads (the way block by block, sections with who builds them, paid and placed blocks, the items waiting to be placed, condition and repairs), requests to cross land and remembered consents, city walls (the ring on the lay of the land, gates, sections with what is paid and placed, the number in the register of buildings), camps (robbers, the leader, the loot, who knows of it, the blocks of the camp and its chest) and the ways of caravans with the pieces already passed. Loading never throws: data of a newer format, a damaged version or an unreadable entry keep the roads read-only (`/kingdomworld admin roads unlock` with a backup). Robbers, merchants of caravans and pack llamas are not saved with chunks (llamas of an earlier run go when loaded): camps and caravans bring them back near players. The format of the kingdom data did not change.

Kingdoms of the world are stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_npc.dat`, format 6 (format 6 added the settlements of the states and the roads planned between them: rank, place, province, names, state, houses, people, food, guard, militia, prosperity, the last day lived; within the same format 6 a settlement keeps the stage and step of its rising, the height of its ground, the founding stores given, its city, when a player was last near and its folded citizens; older data is read without settlements; older formats are read as they are: rulers of format 1 start with an empty memory, places from before format 4 keep the old grid layout): the memories of rulers (decrees, the chronicle, needs, decisions waiting, relations to neighbours, dealings with players), the plan (where the planner stopped, on which pass, whether it is made), the places (position, size, seed, layout, look and wood, places of towns, state, kingdom, stage and step of the appearance, height of the square, why a place was left alone; the towns that stay) and the records of the kingdoms of the world (the mark of a kingdom of the world, names in both alphabets, the ruler with a leaning and traits, towns, the day it appeared and the day of the ruler's last turn). Loading never throws: data of a newer format, a damaged version or an unreadable entry keep them read-only (`/kingdomworld admin npc unlock` with a backup). The category "Founding" (`founding`) is added at the end of the journal of treasuries. The format of the kingdom data did not change. Since stage 14d a place may have the styled layout 3 with its architectural style in `style` and the rank of its guild in the optional key `guild` (`b`, `a`, `s`, `ss`), without a new format number; an older build of the mod keeps such data read-only.

The life of cities is stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_life.dat`, format 1: the ill with how far they got, the last evening of every citizen in a tavern, the festivals of kingdoms with the chronicle of festivals and fairs, the days of the fairs of cities and the lots of goods brought to them, rooms of players in taverns with their point of return before, and the camps marked on atlases. Loading never throws: data of a newer format, a damaged version or an unreadable entry keep it read-only (`/kingdomworld admin life unlock` with a backup). The record of a citizen and the format of the kingdom data did not change.

Diplomacy is stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_diplomacy.dat`, format 2: relations with their reasons, treaties, offers (format 2 added the cession of a province with its price), wars with their sides, allies, calls, score, exhaustion and outcome, tributes, claims of land, cities and thrones for the next stage (format 2 added the provinces a peace hands over; format 1 reads as it is), the chronicle of wars and treaties, when the players of each kingdom were last online and fought, and the times of the wars of rulers of the world. Loading never throws: data of a newer format, a damaged version or an unreadable entry keep diplomacy read-only (`/kingdomworld admin war unlock` with a backup). The "Diplomacy" right goes to the chancellor and "Declare war" stays with the king; old worlds get the first once (upgrade `diplomacy_rights`, the format number of the kingdoms stays). Ledger line "War".

The nobility is stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_nobility.dat`, format 1: houses, titles, courts, demands, plots and spies. Loading never throws: data it cannot understand keeps the nobility read-only (`/kingdomworld admin nobility unlock` with a backup). Ledger line "Nobility".

The files of the kingdoms, the kingdoms of the world, the roads, the provinces and the survey of the land are written to the disk off the server thread: the tag of the data is made on the server thread as before, and compressing and writing happen on a thread of their own in the order of the saves, first into a temporary file `<file>.tmp`, then put in place of the old one in one move. A write that fails leaves the old file, says so at ERROR in the log and is written again at the next save; a stopping server and `save-all flush` wait until the writing has ended. The format of the files does not change.

Provinces are stored apart, in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_provinces.dat`, format 1: tiles of 32 by 32 chunks as runs (`(id << 16) | length`), the table of provinces (middle, kind, neighbours with the kind of the border, the seed of names, the main settlement), holders, the day acquired, holds for buyers, the stage of the plan of the world and the log of the adoption of an old world. The file is written only when something changed. No file: no provinces, and everything is as before. A province whose holder is not among the kingdoms (the data of the kingdoms failed to load or was put back from a copy) stays with that holder and is not saved over (WARN in the log with the number of such provinces); a claim inside another's province stays a claim (WARN). A file that cannot be understood or is newer makes the provinces read-only: the provinces read still protect the land, the file is not overwritten until `/kingdomworld admin land unlock` (a backup is made first). The survey of the land of the plan of states is kept in `<world>/data/kingdomworld_survey.dat`, format 1: the kind of land and the height of the ground of every cell of 64 blocks and how far the survey has got; the file is written as the rows are done and never again once the survey is complete. A survey file that cannot be understood or is newer is not overwritten, and the plan waits until the file is fixed or taken away.

If the data cannot be fully read (a format newer than this version understands, a damaged format version, a failed migration, an unreadable kingdom, citizen, storehouse, treasury or account, or an unreadable file), loading does not throw: the data becomes read-only. What could be read stays available and keeps protecting land, but every change is refused with an ERROR in the log, the citizen simulation stops, storehouses stay closed, and the file is never overwritten. Fix the file or run `/kingdomworld admin data unlock` to leave read-only mode.

### For map mod integrations
